## Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation and Offshore Renewable Energy Management Act {#canadanewfoundland-and-labrador-atlantic-accord-implementation-and-offshore-renewable-energy-management-act .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 1987, c. 3
:::

Assented to 1987-03-25

An Act to implement an agreement between the Government of Canada and
the Government of Newfoundland and Labrador on offshore petroleum
resource management and revenue sharing, to provide for the joint
management of offshore renewable energy by those Governments and to make
related and consequential amendments
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
###### Preamble {#preamble .MarginalNote}

Whereas the Government of Canada and the Government of Newfoundland and
Labrador have entered into the Atlantic Accord and have agreed that
neither Government will introduce amendments to this Act or any
regulation made thereunder without the consent of both Governments:
:::

::: Enacts
Therefore, Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the Senate
and House of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-152536 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation
and Offshore Renewable Energy Management Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 1
- 2014, c. 13, s. 3
- [2024, c. 20, s. 2](#1491053-1529246){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491053-1529246.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-1-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 1"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-152541 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** In this Act,

[[abandoned facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[abandoned facility]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
    [[pipeline]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in section 135,
    installation, facility, equipment or system that has been abandoned
    in accordance with an authorization issued under Part
    III; ([installation abandonnée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Atlantic Accord]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Atlantic Accord]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Memorandum of
    Agreement between the Government of Canada and the Government of the
    Province on offshore petroleum resource management and revenue
    sharing dated February 11, 1985, and includes any amendments
    thereto; ([Accord atlantique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2024, c. 20, s.
    3\]]{.Repealed}

[[Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador benefits plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador benefits
    plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a plan submitted under subsection
    45(2); ([plan de retombées économiques]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Canada-Newfoundland benefits plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Canada-Newfoundland benefits
    plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, 2014, c. 13, s.
    4\]]{.Repealed}

[[Chief Executive Officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Chief Executive Officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Chief
    Executive Officer of the Regulator appointed pursuant to section
    24; ([premier dirigeant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[development plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[development plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a plan submitted
    pursuant to subsection 139(2) for the purpose of obtaining approval
    of the general approach of developing a pool or field as proposed in
    the plan; ([plan de mise en valeur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Federal Government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Federal Government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Governor in
    Council; ([gouvernement fédéral]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Federal Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Federal Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
    Natural Resources; ([ministre fédéral]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[field]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[field]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} means a general surface area underlain or
      appearing to be underlain by one or more pools, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} includes the subsurface regions vertically
      beneath the general surface area referred to in paragraph
      (a); ([champ]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[former regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[former regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the [Canada Oil and
    Gas Land Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1518) made
    pursuant to the Public Lands Grants Act and the [Territorial Lands
    Act](/eng/acts/T-7) and includes orders made pursuant to those
    Regulations; ([anciens règlements]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[fundamental decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fundamental decision]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a decision made
    by the Regulator respecting the exercise of a power or the
    performance of a duty pursuant to a provision of this Act that
    expressly provides for the exercise of the power or the performance
    of the duty subject to sections 31 to 40; ([décision
    majeure]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[gas]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means natural gas and includes all
    substances, other than oil, that are produced in association with
    natural gas; ([gaz]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Federal Government, the
    Provincial Government or both, as the context requires; ([Version
    anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Indigenous peoples of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
    assigned by the definition [[aboriginal peoples of
    Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in subsection 35(2) of the
    [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/); ([peuples autochtones du
    Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Federal Minister, the
    Provincial Minister or both, as the context requires; ([Version
    anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[offshore area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of petroleum, those submarine areas
      lying seaward of the low water mark of the Province and extending,
      at any location, as far as

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any prescribed line, or

      - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if no line is prescribed at that location, the
        outer edge of the continental margin or a distance of two
        hundred nautical miles from the baselines from which the breadth
        of the territorial sea of Canada is measured, whichever is the
        greater, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of offshore renewable energy, the
      areas referred to in paragraph (a) that are not within the
      Province; ([zone extracôtière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"} ou
      [zone]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[offshore renewable energy project]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore renewable energy project]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any
    of the following works and activities:

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any research or assessment conducted in relation
      to the exploitation or potential exploitation of a renewable
      resource to produce an energy product, unless it is conducted by
      or on behalf of a government or educational institution,

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any exploitation of a renewable resource to
      produce an energy product,

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any storage of an energy product produced from a
      renewable resource, and

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any transmission of an energy product produced
      from a renewable resource; ([projet d'énergie renouvelable
      extracôtière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[offshore renewable energy recommendation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[offshore renewable energy
    recommendation]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a recommendation made by
    the Regulator respecting the exercise of a power or the performance
    of a duty under a provision of this Act that expressly provides for
    the exercise of the power or the performance of the duty subject to
    sections 40.1 to 40.3; ([recommandation relative à l'énergie
    renouvelable extracôtière]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[oil]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} crude oil regardless of gravity produced at a
      well head in liquid form, and

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other hydrocarbons, except coal and gas, and,
      without limiting the generality of the foregoing, hydrocarbons
      that may be extracted or recovered from deposits of oil sand,
      bitumen, bituminous sand, oil shale or from any other types of
      deposits on the seabed or subsoil thereof of the offshore
      area; ([pétrole]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[petroleum]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[petroleum]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means oil or
    gas; ([hydrocarbures]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[pool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[pool]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a natural underground reservoir
    containing or appearing to contain an accumulation of petroleum that
    is separated or appears to be separated from any other such
    accumulation; ([gisement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by regulations
    made by the Governor in Council; ([Version anglaise
    seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

[[Province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the province of Newfoundland
    and Labrador; ([province]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Provincial Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Provincial Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canada-Newfoundland
    and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation Newfoundland and
    Labrador Act, R.S.N.L. 1990, c. C-2, as amended from time to
    time; ([loi provinciale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Provincial Government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Provincial Government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the lieutenant
    governor in council of the Province; ([gouvernement
    provincial]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Provincial Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Provincial Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, other than for
    the purposes of Part III.1, the Minister of the Crown in right of
    the Province designated by the Provincial Government as the
    provincial minister for the purposes of this Act; ([ministre
    provincial]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Canada--Newfoundland and
    Labrador Offshore Energy Regulator referred to in section
    9; ([Régie]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[spill-treating agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[spill-treating agent]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, except in section
    161.5, means a spill-treating agent that is on the list established
    under section 14.2 of the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations
    Act](/eng/acts/O-7). ([agent de traitement]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote

- 1994, c. 41, s. 37

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offshore renewable energy project

**[[2.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2.1 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 7,
the Governor in Council may make regulations amending the definition
[[offshore renewable energy project]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined
in section 2, to add or remove any work or activity related to renewable
energy that is carried out in the offshore area.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 4](#1491061-1529267){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491061-1529267.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Construction

**[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty, the
provisions of this Act shall not be interpreted as providing a basis for
any claim by or on behalf of any province in respect of any interest in
or legislative jurisdiction over any offshore area or any living or
non-living resources of any offshore area.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Precedence over other Acts of Parliament

**[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** In case of any
inconsistency or conflict between

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} this Act or any regulations made thereunder, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other Act of Parliament that applies to the
  offshore area or any regulations made under that Act, except the
  [Labrador Inuit Land Claims Agreement Act](/eng/acts/L-4.3),

this Act and the regulations made thereunder take precedence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 4
- 1992, c. 35, s. 44
- 2005, c. 27, s. 18
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Prescribing Limits of Offshore Area and Settlement Procedure for Disputes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-152585 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject
  to section 7, the Governor in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing lines enclosing areas adjacent to the
    Province for the purpose of subparagraph (a)(i) of the definition
    [[offshore area]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in section 2; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} amending paragraph (b) of that definition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Issue of charts

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister may cause charts to be issued
  setting out the offshore area or any portion thereof as may be set out
  consistent with the nature and scale of the chart.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In any legal or other proceedings, a chart purporting
  to be issued by or under the authority of the Federal Minister is
  conclusive proof of the limits of the offshore area or portion thereof
  set out in the chart without proof of the signature or official
  character of the person purporting to have issued the chart.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 5
- [2024, c. 20, s. 5](#1491063-1529270){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491063-1529270.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this
  section,

  [[agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement between the
      Government of Canada and the government of a province respecting
      resource management and revenue sharing in relation to activities
      respecting the exploration for or the production of petroleum, or
      respecting offshore renewable energy projects, carried out on any
      frontier lands; ([accord]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[frontier lands]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[frontier lands]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means lands that belong to
      Her Majesty in right of Canada, or in respect of which Her Majesty
      in right of Canada has the right to dispose of or exploit the
      natural resources and that are situated in

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Yukon, the Northwest Territories, Nunavut or
        Sable Island, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} those submarine areas, not within a province,
        adjacent to the coast of Canada and extending throughout the
        natural prolongation of the land territory of Canada to the
        outer edge of the continental margin or to a distance of two
        hundred nautical miles from the baselines from which the breadth
        of the territorial sea of Canada is measured, whichever is the
        greater. ([terres domaniales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disputes between neighbouring provinces

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a dispute between the Province and any other
  province that is a party to an agreement arises in relation to a line
  or portion thereof prescribed or to be prescribed for the purpose of
  the definition [[offshore area]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in section 2 and
  the Government of Canada is unable, by means of negotiation, to bring
  about a resolution of the dispute within a reasonable time, the
  dispute shall, at such time as the Federal Minister deems appropriate,
  be referred to an impartial person, tribunal or body and settled by
  means of the procedure determined in accordance with subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedures determined by Federal
  Minister

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the person,
  tribunal or body to which a dispute is to be referred, the
  constitution and membership of any tribunal or body and the procedures
  for the settlement of a dispute shall be determined by the Federal
  Minister after consultation with the provinces concerned in the
  dispute.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Principles of international law to
  apply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the procedure for the settlement of a dispute
  pursuant to this section involves arbitration, the arbitrator shall
  apply the principles of international law governing maritime boundary
  delimitation, with such modifications as the circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Provincial Minister not
  required before regulation made

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 7, where a dispute is settled
  pursuant to this section and a regulation under subsection 5(1)
  prescribing the line in relation to which the dispute arose is made in
  accordance with the settlement, the regulation is not subject to the
  procedure set out in section 7 with respect to the portion of the line
  to which the dispute related.

- 1987, c. 3, s. 6

- 2002, c. 7, s. 108(E)

:::

## [Condition Precedent for Certain Regulations]{.HTitleText1} {#h-152610 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial Minister's approval

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before a
  regulation is made under section 2.1, subsection 5(1), section 29.1,
  subsection 41(7), section 56.1 or section 64, subsection 67(2),
  section 96.1, subsection 96.2(2), section 118, subsection 122(1),
  125(1), 149(1), 162(2.3), 163(1.02), 183.19(6) or 183.25(1), section
  183.29, subsection 183.3(3) or 202.01(1) or section 203, the Federal
  Minister shall consult the Provincial Minister with respect to the
  proposed regulation and the regulation shall not be made without the
  Provincial Minister's approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Provincial minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before a regulation is made under subsection
  205.001(3) or (4) or 205.124(1), the Federal Minister shall consult
  the minister of the government of the Province who is responsible for
  occupational health and safety with respect to the proposed regulation
  and no regulation shall be so made without the approval of that
  minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 7
- 2014, c. 13, s. 5
- 2015, c. 4, ss. 38, 117
- [2024, c. 20, s. 7](#1491067-1529276){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491067-1529276.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-7-20160227.html "Link to previous version of section 7"){.PITLink}
:::

## [Non-Application of Atlantic Accord]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1531637 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of Atlantic Accord

**[[7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.1 .sectionLabel}** For greater certainty,
the Atlantic Accord does not apply to offshore renewable energy
resources.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 8](#1491069-1531636){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491069-1531636.html"}
:::

## [Application]{.HTitleText1} {#h-152618 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This Act
  applies within the offshore area.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excluded legislation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 101, the [Canada Petroleum
  Resources Act](/eng/acts/C-8.5) and the [Canada Oil and Gas Operations
  Act](/eng/acts/O-7) and Part 5 of the [Canadian Energy Regulator
  Act](/eng/acts/C-15.1) and any regulations made under them do not
  apply within the offshore area.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 8
- 1992, c. 35, s. 45
- [2024, c. 20, s. 9](#1491071-1529278){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491071-1529278.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 8"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Joint Management]{.HTitleText1} {#h-152626 .Part}

### [Establishment of Regulator]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152627 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jointly established Regulator

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There is
  established by the joint operation of this Act and the Provincial Act
  a board to be known as the Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore
  Energy Regulator.

- [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2024, c. 20, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} A reference in any contract, document, instrument,
  proclamation, by-law or order in council to the Canada-Newfoundland
  Offshore Petroleum Board or the Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador
  Offshore Petroleum Board is, unless the context otherwise requires, to
  be read as a reference to the Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador
  Offshore Energy Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Treated as Provincial entity

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), the Regulator
  shall for all purposes be treated as having been established by or
  under a law of the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of corporation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator has the legal powers and capacities of
  a corporation incorporated under the [Canada Business Corporations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-44), including those set out in section 21 of the
  [Interpretation Act](/eng/acts/I-21).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Dissolution of Regulator

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may be dissolved only by the joint
  operation of an Act of Parliament and an Act of the Legislature of the
  Province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 9

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulator consisting of seven members

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator shall consist of seven members.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal and Provincial appointees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Three members of the Regulator are to be appointed by
  the Federal Government, three by the Provincial Government and the
  Chairperson of the Regulator is to be appointed by both the Federal
  Government and the Provincial Government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vice-chairpersons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} One or two members of the Regulator may be designated
  to be vice-chairpersons of the Regulator if they are so designated by
  both the Federal Government and the Provincial Government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation by both governments

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The designation of a vice-chairperson of the
  Regulator pursuant to subsection (3) is effective after both
  governments have each made the designation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternate members

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Each government may appoint one alternate member to
  act as a member of the Regulator in the absence of any of the members
  of the Regulator appointed by that government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Joint appointees

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (2) or (5), any member or
  alternate member of the Regulator may be appointed by both the Federal
  Government and the Provincial Government.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 10
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530459){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530459.html"}
- [2024, c. 20, s. 102(E)](#1491484-1531099){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491484-1531099.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-10-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 10"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Qualifications

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  member of the Regulator shall, during the term of office of that
  member on the Regulator, be employed in the Public Service of Canada
  or be a civil servant in the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this section,

  [[civil servant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[civil servant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      the Provincial Act; ([fonctionnaire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Public Service of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Public Service of Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
      given the expression [[public service]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} in
      the [Federal Public Sector Labour Relations
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3), and includes any portion of the federal
      public administration designated by order in council pursuant to
      this subsection and for the purposes of subsection (1) as part of
      the Public Service of Canada. ([administration
      fédérale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 11
- 2003, c. 22, s. 117(E)
- 2017, c. 9, s. 55
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530461){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530461.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20170619.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed consultation between governments
re Chairperson

- **[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Consultation between the two
  governments with respect to the selection of the Chairperson of the
  Regulator shall be deemed to commence

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} six months prior to the expiration of the term of
    office of the incumbent Chairperson, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where applicable, on the date of receipt by the
    Regulator of notice of the death, resignation or termination of
    appointment of the incumbent Chairperson,

  whichever occurs earlier.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no agreement on Chairperson

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the two governments fail to agree on the
  appointment of the Chairperson of the Regulator within three months
  after the commencement of consultation between the governments, the
  Chairperson shall be selected by a panel, consisting of three members
  and constituted in accordance with this section, unless, at any time
  prior to the selection of the Chairperson by the panel, the two
  governments agree on the appointment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of members of panel

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} One member of the panel shall be appointed by each
  government within thirty days after the expiration of the three months
  referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairperson of panel

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The chairperson of the panel shall be appointed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} jointly by the two members of the panel appointed
    pursuant to subsection (3) within thirty days after the later of the
    two appointments made pursuant to that subsection; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the two members of the panel fail to agree on
    the appointment of the chairperson of the panel within the 30-day
    period referred to in paragraph (a), by the Chief Justice of
    Newfoundland and Labrador within 30 days after the expiration of
    that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Selection of Chairperson of Regulator
  within 60 days

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the Regulator shall be selected by
  the panel within 60 days after the appointment of the chairperson of
  the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision of panel binding

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The decision of the panel selecting a Chairperson of
  the Regulator is final and binding on both governments.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 12
- 2014, c. 13, ss. 7, 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530463){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530463.html"}
- [2024, c. 20, s. 102(E)](#1491484-1531110){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491484-1531110.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-12-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 12"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Salaries of joint appointees

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 15, the salary
  and other terms and conditions of the appointment of the Chairperson
  of the Regulator or any other member or alternate member appointed by
  both governments, including the effective date of the appointment,
  shall be fixed by an order of the Federal Government and an order of
  the Provincial Government after agreement has been reached by both
  governments on the salary and other terms and conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Salaries of separate appointees

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The salary and other terms and conditions of the
  appointment of any member appointed by either the Federal Government
  or the Provincial Government shall be agreed on by both governments.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 13
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530468){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530468.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity of Chairperson

**[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator shall
designate a member to act as Chairperson of the Regulator during any
absence or incapacity of the Chairperson or vacancy in the office of
Chairperson, and that person, while acting as Chairperson, has and may
exercise all of the powers and perform all of the duties and functions
of the Chairperson.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 14
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530470){.wb-lbx

:::

[Previous
Version](section-14-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 14"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2024, c. 20, s.
  11\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2024, c. 20, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of office

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson and members of the Regulator shall be
  appointed for terms of six years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Good behaviour

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A member of the Regulator, including the Chairperson,
  shall hold office during good behaviour, but may be removed for cause

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the member is appointed by either government,
    by that government; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the member is appointed by both governments,
    by both governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Re-appointment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On the expiration of a term of office, the
  Chairperson or a member of the Regulator is eligible for
  re-appointment for one or more further terms.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 15
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)

  ajax-after="1491484-1531115.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-15-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conflict of interest guidelines

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Members of the Regulator, including
  the Chairperson, and the Chief Executive Officer appointed pursuant to
  section 24 shall be subject to conflict of interest guidelines
  established jointly by the Federal Minister and Provincial Minister
  and are not subject to any conflict of interest guidelines established
  by the Federal Government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insurance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall purchase and maintain insurance
  for the benefit of a person who is a present or former member, officer
  or employee of the Regulator, and the heirs or legal representatives
  of that person, against any liability incurred by that person in the
  capacity as such a member, officer or employee, except where the
  liability relates to a failure to act honestly and in good faith with
  regard to the best interests of the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expenditures for insurance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the expenditures of the
  Regulator associated with purchasing and maintaining the insurance
  referred to in subsection (2) shall form part of the budget or revised
  budget of the Regulator in respect of a fiscal year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to indemnify

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (2), where the Regulator
  has established to the satisfaction of the Federal Minister the
  impossibility of purchasing and maintaining the insurance referred to
  in subsection (2), the Government of Canada shall, subject to
  subsection (6), indemnify a person who is a present or former member,
  officer or employee of the Regulator, or the heirs or legal
  representatives of that person, against all costs, charges and
  expenses, including an amount paid to settle an action or satisfy a
  judgment, reasonably incurred in respect of any civil, criminal or
  administrative action or proceeding to which that person is a party by
  reason of being or having been such a member, officer or employee, if
  that person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} acted honestly and in good faith with a view to the
    best interests of the Regulator; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of any criminal or administrative
    action or proceeding that is enforced by a monetary penalty,
    believed, on reasonable grounds, that the conduct in issue was
    lawful.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional Coverage

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Regulator has purchased and maintained
  insurance referred to in subsection (2), the Government of Canada
  shall indemnify a person referred to in that subsection, or the heirs
  or legal representatives of that person, for any liability incurred by
  that person in accordance with this section to the extent that the
  insurance purchased for the benefit of that person does not cover such
  liability.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount to settle an action

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Government of Canada is not obliged to indemnify
  anyone pursuant to subsection (4) against an amount paid to settle an
  action unless the amount so paid was approved by the Government of
  Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Both governments share costs of
  indemnification

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where the Government of the Province has indemnified
  a person referred to in subsection (4), or the heirs or legal
  representatives of that person, pursuant to section 16 of the
  Provincial Act, the Government of Canada may pay to the Government of
  the Province one-half of the amount so indemnified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payable out of Consolidated Revenue
  Fund

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Any amount payable in respect of indemnification
  under this section may be paid out of the Consolidated Revenue Fund.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 16
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530473){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530473.html"}
:::

### [Functions of Regulator]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152734 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Functions of Regulator

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator shall perform such duties and functions as are conferred or
  imposed on the Regulator by or pursuant to the Atlantic Accord or this
  Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proposed amendments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may make recommendations to both
  governments with respect to proposed amendments to this Act, the
  Provincial Act and any regulations made under those Acts.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 17
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530490){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530490.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation with Indigenous peoples of
Canada

**[[17.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17.1 .sectionLabel}** His Majesty in right
of Canada or in right of the Province may rely on the Regulator for the
purposes of consulting with the Indigenous peoples of Canada respecting
any potential adverse impact of a work or activity in the offshore area
on existing Aboriginal and treaty rights recognized and affirmed by
section 35 of the [Constitution Act, 1982](/eng/Const/) and the
Regulator may, on behalf of His Majesty, if appropriate, accommodate any
adverse impacts on those rights.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 12](#1491077-1529298){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491077-1529298.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to information

- **[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister are entitled to access to
  any information or documentation relating to petroleum resource and
  renewable energy activities in the offshore area that is provided for
  the purposes of this Act or any regulation made under it and such
  information or documentation shall, on the request of either Minister,
  be disclosed to that Minister without requiring the consent of the
  party who provided the information or documentation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable provision

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Section 119 applies, with such modifications as the
  circumstances require, in respect of any disclosure of information or
  documentation or the production or giving of evidence relating thereto
  by a Minister as if the references in that section to the
  administration or enforcement of a Part of this Act included
  references to the administration or enforcement of the Provincial Act
  or any Part thereof.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 18
- [2024, c. 20, s. 13](#1491079-1529301){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491079-1529301.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 18"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Administration]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152745 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meetings of Regulator

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** A meeting of the
Regulator shall be held

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} once every month unless the members of the Regulator
  unanimously agree to defer such a meeting; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} at any other time

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at the call of the Chairperson of the Regulator,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} on the request of any two members of the
    Regulator, or

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} on the request of the Federal Minister or the
    Provincial Minister to review any matter referred to it by that
    Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 19
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530493){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530493.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Quorum

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Four
  members constitute a quorum of the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Majority vote

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, in the absence of unanimous agreement, a vote
  is required to be taken in respect of a decision of the Regulator, the
  decision shall be made on the basis of a majority vote of the members
  of the Regulator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 20
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530500){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530500.html"}

  ajax-after="1491484-1531119.html"}
:::

Version](section-20-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 20"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Location of offices and staff

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** The principal office and
staff of the Regulator shall be located in the Province.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 21
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530507){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530507.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-21-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 21"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Storage of information

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator shall
establish, maintain and operate a facility in the Province for the
storage and curatorship of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} all petroleum-related geophysical records and
  geological and hydrocarbon samples relating to the offshore area; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} all geological, geophysical, geotechnical and
  environmental records and geological and geotechnical samples relating
  to offshore renewable energy.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 22
- [2024, c. 20, s. 14](#1491081-1529310){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491081-1529310.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-22-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 22"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}By-laws and guidelines

**[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** Subject to this Act and
the Atlantic Accord, the Regulator may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} make by-laws respecting

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the members, officers and employees of the
    Regulator,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the attendance and participation, including voting
    rights, at meetings of the Regulator of alternate members of the
    Regulator appointed pursuant to subsection 10(5),

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the manner of appointing the officers and
    employees of the Regulator on the basis of selection according to
    merit, including the holding of open competitions therefor,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the procedures to be followed in the performance
    of any of the duties and functions of the Regulator,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of meetings of the Regulator,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the manner of dealing with matters and business
    before the Regulator, and

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} generally, the carrying on of the work of the
    Regulator and the management of the internal affairs thereof; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} establish conflict of interest guidelines respecting
  persons employed by the Regulator pursuant to subsection 25(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 23
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530510){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530510.html"}
- [2024, c. 20, s. 102(E)](#1491484-1531121){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491484-1531121.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-23-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chief Executive Officer

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There
  shall be a Chief Executive Officer of the Regulator who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} where both the Federal Government and the
    Provincial Government appoint the Chairperson as Chief Executive
    Officer, is the Chairperson of the Regulator; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, is to be appointed by the
    Regulator by means of an open competition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval required

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The appointment of a Chief Executive Officer pursuant
  to paragraph (1)(b) is subject to the approval of both governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Panel to choose Chief Executive Officer
  in the absence of agreement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where either government fails to make an appointment
  pursuant to paragraph (1)(a) or to approve the appointment of a Chief
  Executive Officer pursuant to paragraph (1)(b), the Chief Executive
  Officer shall be appointed by both the Federal Government and the
  Provincial Government after having been selected in accordance with
  section 12 and that section applies, with such modifications as the
  circumstances require, to the selection of the Chief Executive
  Officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsection 13(1)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection 13(1) applies, with such modifications as
  the circumstances require, to the appointment of the Chief Executive
  Officer pursuant to paragraph (1)(a) or subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence or incapacity of Chief
  Executive Officer

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall designate a person to act as
  Chief Executive Officer during any absence or incapacity of that
  Officer or vacancy in the office of Chief Executive Officer and that
  person, while acting as Chief Executive Officer, has and may exercise
  all the powers and perform all of the duties and functions of that
  office.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 24
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530512){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530512.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-24-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 24"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staff of the Regulator

- **[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator may, on the recommendation of the Chief Executive Officer,
  employ such other officers and such employees as are necessary to
  properly perform the duties and functions of the Regulator under this
  Act and the Atlantic Accord.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Method of selection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The appointment of every person employed pursuant to
  subsection (1) shall be based on selection according to merit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in subsection (4), every person
  employed pursuant to subsection (1) is deemed not to be employed in
  the federal public administration or the public service of the
  Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mobility of staff

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of being eligible for appointment to
  a position in the public service by an appointment process under the
  [Public Service Employment Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person who, immediately prior to being employed
    by the Regulator, was employed in the public service shall be deemed
    to be a person employed in the public service in the Department of
    Natural Resources in the location where that person is performing
    duties for the Regulator and in a position of an occupational nature
    and at a level equivalent to the position in which that person is
    employed by the Regulator; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person who, immediately prior to being employed
    by the Regulator, was not employed in the public service shall, two
    years after being employed by the Regulator, be deemed to be a
    person employed in the public service in the Department of Natural
    Resources in the location where that person is performing duties for
    the Regulator and in a position of an occupational nature and at a
    level equivalent to the position in which that person is employed by
    the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Newfoundland and
  Labrador legislation

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} Newfoundland and Labrador social legislation as
  defined in subsection 205.001(1), the provisions of the Labour
  Relations Act, R.S.N.L. 1990, c. L-1, as amended from time to time,
  and the provisions of the Occupational Health and Safety Act, R.S.N.L.
  1990, c. O-3, as amended from time to time, and any regulations made
  under that legislation or those Acts, apply to persons employed under
  subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of [Canada Labour
  Code](/eng/acts/L-2)

  [(4.2)]{.lawlabel} Despite section 4 and subsections 123(1) and 168(1)
  of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), that Act does not apply to
  persons employed under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[public
  service]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[public
  service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in the [Federal
  Public Sector Labour Relations Act](/eng/acts/P-33.3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Auditor

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator shall
appoint an auditor, for such term as is set by the Regulator, for the
purposes of auditing the financial statements of the Regulator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 26
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530525){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530525.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Audit and evaluation committee

- **[[26.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall appoint an audit
  and evaluation committee consisting of not fewer than three members of
  the Regulator and fix the duties and functions of the committee and
  may, by by-law, provide for the payment of expenses to the members of
  the committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Internal audit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to any other duties and functions that it
  is required to perform, the audit and evaluation committee shall cause
  internal audits to be conducted to ensure that the officers and
  employees of the Regulator act in accordance with management systems
  and controls established by the Regulator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 9
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530526){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530526.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26.1-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 26.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Budget

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chief Executive Officer shall, in respect of each fiscal year, prepare
  a budget for the Regulator sufficient to permit the Regulator to
  properly exercise its powers and perform its duties and functions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission to governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Following approval of the budget by the Regulator,
  the budget shall be submitted to the Federal Minister and the
  Provincial Minister, at such time as may be specified by each
  Minister, for their consideration and approval.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revised budget

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where it appears that the actual aggregate of the
  expenditures of the Regulator in respect of any fiscal year is likely
  to be substantially greater or less than that estimated in its budget
  in respect of that fiscal year, the Regulator shall submit to both
  Ministers for their consideration and approval a revised budget in
  respect of that fiscal year containing such particulars as may be
  requested by either Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of operating costs

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4.1), the Government of Canada
  shall pay one-half of the aggregate of the expenditures set out in the
  budget or revised budget in respect of each fiscal year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of specific activities

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Executive Officer may include in the
  budget or revised budget expenditures associated with specific
  requirements of one government that are to be paid entirely by that
  government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appropriation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any other Act of Parliament that
  appropriates moneys for the payment required by subsection (4), the
  sums required for such payment shall be paid out of the Consolidated
  Revenue Fund from time to time as required.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 27
- [2024, c. 20, s. 16](#1491085-1529318){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491085-1529318.html"}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to books and accounts

**[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
18(2), both the Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister are
entitled to have access to the books and accounts of the Regulator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 28

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

- **[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Regulator shall, in respect of each fiscal year, prepare a report and
  submit it to the Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister not
  later than ninety days after the expiration of that fiscal year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Each annual report submitted under subsection (1)
  shall contain an audited financial statement and a description of the
  activities of the Regulator, including those relating to occupational
  health and safety, during the fiscal year covered by the report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling of report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister shall cause the annual report
  referred to in this section

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to be laid before each House of Parliament on any
    of the first fifteen days on which that House is sitting after the
    day the report is submitted to the Federal Minister; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} where it is not possible to cause the report to be
    laid pursuant to paragraph (a) within thirty days after the day the
    report is submitted to the Federal Minister, to be published within
    that thirty day period.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 29

### [Cost Recovery]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152842 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations respecting fees, etc.

- **[[29.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 7, the Governor
  in Council may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the fees or charges, or the method of
    calculating the fees or charges, to be paid for the provision, by
    the Regulator, of a service or product under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the fees or charges, or the method of
    calculating the fees or charges, in respect of any of the
    Regulator's activities under this Act or the [Impact Assessment
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75), that are to be paid by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person who makes an application for an
      authorization under paragraph 138(1)(b) or subsection 138.01(1) or
      an application under subsection 139(2), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the holder of an operating licence issued under
      paragraph 138(1)(a) or an authorization issued under paragraph
      138(1)(b) or subsection 138.01(1); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the refund of all or part of any fee or
    charge referred to in paragraph (a) or (b), or the method of
    calculating that refund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amounts not to exceed cost

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The amounts of the fees or charges referred to in
  paragraph (1)(a) shall not exceed the cost of providing the services
  or products.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amounts not to exceed cost

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The amounts of the fees or charges referred to in
  paragraph (1)(b) shall not exceed the cost of the Regulator's
  activities under this Act or the [Impact Assessment
  Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 4, s. 39
- [2024, c. 20, s. 17](#1491087-1529323){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491087-1529323.html"}
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530548){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530548.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.1-20160227.html "Link to previous version of section 29.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of [Service Fees
Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4)

**[[29.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.2 .sectionLabel}** The [Service Fees
Act](/eng/acts/S-8.4) does not apply to any fees or charges payable in
accordance with regulations made under section 29.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2015, c. 4, s. 39
- 2017, c. 20, s. 454
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-29.2-20160227.html "Link to previous version of section 29.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remittance of fees and charges: shared

- **[[29.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), one half
  of the amounts of the fees and charges obtained in accordance with
  regulations made under section 29.1 shall be paid to the credit of the
  Receiver General and the other half shall be paid to the credit of Her
  Majesty in right of the Province, in the time and manner prescribed
  under those regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remittance of fees and charges to the
  Province

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the fees and charges referred to in subsection (1)
  are related to the powers, duties or functions of the Regulator for
  the administration of the pricing mechanisms for greenhouse gas
  emissions referred to in subsection 164.3(1), they shall be paid in
  their entirety to the credit of Her Majesty in right of the Province.

- 2015, c. 4, s. 39
- [2018, c. 27, s. 176](#1039112-1127480){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1039112-1127480.html"}

  ajax-after="1491355-1530550.html"}

### [Decisions in Relation to Offshore Management]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152865 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulator's decisions final

**[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** Subject to this Act, the
exercise of a power or the performance of a duty by the Regulator
pursuant to this Act is final and not subject to the review or approval
of either government or either Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 30
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530557){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530557.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-30-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 30"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Petroleum-related Decisions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1531521 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of fundamental decisions

- **[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a fundamental decision is made by the Regulator, the Regulator shall,
  forthwith after making the decision, give written notice of that
  decision to the Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advice by Ministers to Regulator

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within thirty days after receipt of a notice of a
  fundamental decision pursuant to subsection (1), the Federal Minister
  and the Provincial Minister shall each advise, in writing, the
  Regulator and each other whether that Minister approves or disapproves
  that decision and where the Regulator does not receive the advice
  within those thirty days, the Regulator shall be deemed, for the
  purposes of section 32, to be advised, in writing, on the expiration
  of that period, of the approval of that decision by that Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 31
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530565){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530565.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-31-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 31"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for implementation of
fundamental decision

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  fundamental decision shall not be implemented unless the Regulator is
  advised, in writing, that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} both the Federal Minister and the Provincial
    Minister approve that decision; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the Minister having authority in
    relation to that decision, as determined under section 34, approves
    that decision and, where the other Minister has exercised the power
    to suspend the approval of that decision pursuant to section 39,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the period of suspension referred to in that
      section has expired, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} agreement is reached between both Ministers to
      approve that decision,

    whichever occurs first.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with advice or governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the conditions referred to in subsection (1)
  have been satisfied in respect of a fundamental decision, that
  decision shall be implemented forthwith by the Regulator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 32
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530568){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530568.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-32-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 32"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** In this section and
sections 34 to 37,

[[security of supply]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[security of supply]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in respect of any
    period, means the anticipation of self-sufficiency during each of
    the five calendar years in that period, taking into account the
    aggregate of anticipated additions to producing capacity and
    anticipated adjustments to refining capacity; ([sécurité des
    approvisionnements]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[self-sufficiency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[self-sufficiency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a volume of suitable
    crude oil and equivalent substances available from domestic Canadian
    hydrocarbon producing capacity that is adequate to supply the total
    feedstock requirements of Canadian refineries necessary to satisfy
    the total refined product requirements of Canada, excluding those
    feedstock requirements necessary to produce specialty refined
    products; ([autosuffisance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[suitable crude oil and equivalent substances]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[suitable crude oil and equivalent
    substances]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means those substances that are
    appropriate for processing in Canadian refineries and that are
    potentially deliverable to Canadian refineries. ([pétrole brut et
    substances assimilées acceptables]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Ministers prior to
self-sufficiency and security of supply

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  in respect of any period referred to in subsection 35(2) or (3), a
  determination is made that self-sufficiency and security of supply do
  not exist, the Federal Minister has authority in relation to any
  fundamental decision, other than a fundamental decision referred to in
  subsection (2), made during that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Development plan approval

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the Provincial Minister
  has authority in relation to a fundamental decision referred to in
  paragraph 139(4)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Federal Minister's power to override
  development plan approval

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the approval or disapproval by the Provincial
  Minister of a fundamental decision referred to in paragraph 139(4)(a)
  would unreasonably delay the attainment of self-sufficiency or
  security of supply, the Federal Minister may substitute therefor the
  approval or disapproval, as the case may be, of the Federal Minister,
  and where the Federal Minister does so, that Minister shall, for the
  purposes of this Act, be considered to have authority in relation to
  that fundamental decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Ministers after
  self-sufficiency and security of supply

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where, in respect of any period referred to in
  subsection 35(3), a determination is made that self-sufficiency and
  security of supply exist, the Provincial Minister has authority in
  relation to any fundamental decision made during that period.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination binding

- **[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this Act, where a determination referred to in
  subsection 34(1) or (4) is made by both governments or by a panel
  pursuant to section 36 or 37 or is deemed to have been made pursuant
  to subsection (2), it is final and binding for the duration of the
  period in respect of which it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 34, the first period
  shall commence on January 1, 1986 and terminate on December 31, 1990
  and, in respect of that period, a determination shall be deemed to
  have been made, for all purposes of this Act, that self-sufficiency
  and security of supply do not exist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent periods

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of section 34, each period following
  the period referred to in subsection (2) shall commence on the
  expiration of the period immediately preceding that period and shall
  be for a duration of five successive calendar years.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no agreement on determinations re
self-sufficiency

- **[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Consultation between the two
  governments with respect to the making of a determination referred to
  in subsection 34(1) or (4) shall be deemed to commence one year prior
  to the expiration of every period in respect of which such a
  determination is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of panel members

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the two governments fail to agree on a
  determination referred to in subsection (1) within three months after
  the commencement of consultation between the governments, the
  determination shall be made by a panel consisting of three members,
  constituted in accordance with subsections 12(3) and (4), within sixty
  days after the appointment of the chairperson of the panel unless, at
  any time prior thereto, the two governments agree on the
  determination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 36
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-36-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 36"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of unreasonable delay

- **[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  within sixty days after an approval or disapproval by the Provincial
  Minister pursuant to subsection 34(2), the two governments fail to
  agree whether the approval or disapproval would unreasonably delay the
  attainment of self-sufficiency or security of supply, that
  determination shall be made by a panel consisting of three members
  constituted in accordance with subsection (2), within forty-five days
  after the appointment of the chairperson of the panel.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Constitution of panel

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), one member of the
  panel shall be appointed by each government within thirty days after
  the sixty days referred to in subsection (1) and the chairperson of
  the panel shall be appointed in accordance with subsection 12(4) and
  for that purpose, subsection 12(4) applies, with such modifications as
  the circumstances require.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 37
- 2014, c. 13, s. 52(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-37-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 37"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination not subject to review

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** Where a determination
referred to in section 36 or 37 is made by a panel pursuant to that
section, that determination is not subject to be reviewed or set aside
by any government, court or other body.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspensive vetoes

- **[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister who does not have authority in relation to a fundamental
  decision, as determined under section 34, may, on giving written
  notice to the Regulator and the Minister who has such authority,
  suspend, during a period of ninety days, the approval of the
  fundamental decision by the Minister who has that authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commencement of period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The period of ninety days referred to in
  subsection (1) commences on the day the Regulator is advised, in
  accordance with subsection 31(2), of the approval of the fundamental
  decision by the Minister having authority in relation to the
  fundamental decision.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 39
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530579){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530579.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-39-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 39"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Supply shortfall

- **[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision
  of this Act, in the event of a sudden domestic or import supply
  shortfall of suitable crude oil and equivalent substances, the
  Regulator shall, on request by the Federal Minister, cause production
  of suitable crude oil and equivalent substances to be increased,
  consistent with good oil field practice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Canada's obligations under IEA

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding any other provision of this Act,
  where the Government of Canada has obligations with respect to the
  allocation of petroleum pursuant to the Agreement On An International
  Energy Program dated November 18, 1974, the Regulator shall, where
  directed to do so by the Federal Minister and during the period that
  those obligations continue, take such measures as are necessary to
  comply with those obligations and as are fair and equitable in
  relation to other hydrocarbon producing regions of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 40
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530587){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530587.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-40-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 40"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Decisions Related to Offshore Renewable Energy]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1531522 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulator's recommendation

- **[[40.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall notify the
  Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister in writing of its
  offshore renewable energy recommendation as soon as practicable after
  deciding to make the recommendation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministers' decisions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister
  shall, within 60 days after receiving the recommendation, notify the
  Regulator in writing of their respective decision to approve the
  recommendation, with or without variations, or to reject it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional 30 days

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), on written notice by either
  Minister to the Regulator and the other Minister, both Ministers shall
  have an additional 30 days to notify the Regulator of their respective
  decision under that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No time limit

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), the Federal Minister or the
  Provincial Minister may notify the Regulator of their respective
  decision with respect to an offshore renewable energy recommendation
  to make a call for bids beyond the period of 60 days referred to in
  that subsection.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 19](#1491094-1531533){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491094-1531533.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

**[[40.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40.2 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator shall
publish in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) any notice of
decision referred to in subsection 40.1(2), unless the decision is to
reject an offshore renewable energy recommendation to make a call for
bids.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 19](#1491094-1531537){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491094-1531537.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of Ministers' approval

**[[40.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40.3 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator shall
only exercise a power or perform a duty subject to this section and
sections 40.1 and 40.2 if it has been the subject of an offshore
renewable energy recommendation that was approved by both Ministers and
with the same variations, if any. In that case, the Regulator shall
exercise the power or perform the duty as soon as practicable after it
receives the notices referred to in subsection 40.1(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 19](#1491094-1531541){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491094-1531541.html"}
:::

### [Regional Security of Supply]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152930 .Subheading}

Definition of [[shortfall of petroleum deliveries in the
Province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section [[shortfall of petroleum deliveries in
  the Province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means deliveries of petroleum that
  are inadequate to supply, on commercial terms,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the end use consumption and feedstock requirements
    of industrial facilities that are in place in the Province on the
    day of the coming into force of this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the feedstock requirements of the refining
    facilities at Come-by-Chance if those facilities were operating at
    capacity on the day of the coming into force of this Act or any
    refining facility constructed in the Province to replace those
    facilities; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the feedstock requirements of any refining facility
    located in the Province that was not in place on the coming into
    force of this Act, other than a facility referred to in paragraph
    (b), if the feedstock requirements required to satisfy the demand of
    industrial capacity, on the day of the coming into force of this
    Act, in Nova Scotia, New Brunswick, Prince Edward Island and
    Newfoundland and Labrador have been met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice by Provincial Minister to
  holders of production licences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a shortfall of petroleum deliveries in
  the Province, the Provincial Minister may, after consulting with the
  Federal Minister, give notice to holders of production licences in the
  offshore area that the facilities in paragraphs (1)(a), (b) and (c)
  that are specified in the notice have, during the term of the notice,
  the first option to acquire, on commercial terms, petroleum produced
  in the offshore area unless a sales contract with respect to that
  petroleum has been entered into prior to the giving of the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Later contracts subject to notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any contract entered into after the giving of the
  notice referred to in subsection (2) shall be deemed to be varied or
  suspended to the extent necessary to give effect to that notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Term of notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The term of a notice given under subsection (2) is
  the period during which a shortfall of petroleum deliveries in the
  Province continues to exist.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Arbitration in case of dispute whether
  shortfall exists

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Federal Minister or a holder of a
  production licence to whom a notice has been given under
  subsection (2) does not agree with the Provincial Minister that a
  shortfall of petroleum deliveries in the Province exists or continues
  to exist, the matter shall be referred to arbitration in the manner
  prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice ceases to have effect

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where it is determined pursuant to arbitration that a
  shortfall of petroleum deliveries in the Province does not exist or
  continue to exist, the notice given under subsection (2) shall be
  deemed to be revoked and ceases to have effect on the date on which
  the determination is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 7, the Governor in Council may
  make regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of this
  section and, without limiting the generality of the foregoing, may
  make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining the expression [[commercial
    terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} or providing for arbitration to
    establish commercial terms in any particular case;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} governing, for the purposes of this section,
    arbitration and the making of arbitration orders and appeals from
    and enforcement of arbitration orders; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner of exercising a first option
    to acquire that is granted pursuant to a notice given under
    subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 41
- 2014, c. 13, s. 11
:::

Version](section-41-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 41"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Ministerial Directives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-152954 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial directives

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister may jointly issue to the
  Regulator written directives in relation to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fundamental decisions;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} offshore renewable energy recommendations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} decisions made by the Regulator respecting the
    exercise of a power pursuant to paragraph 56(1)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} public reviews conducted pursuant to section 44;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador benefits plans
    and any of their provisions;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} the principles referred to in section 96.6;

  - [(d.2)]{.lawlabel} the development of guidelines and interpretation
    notes issued under subsection 151.1(1) and section 183.27; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} studies to be conducted by the Regulator and advice
    with respect to policy issues to be given by the Regulator to the
    Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Occupational health and safety
  directives

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister, on the recommendation of the
  Minister of Labour, and the minister of the government of the Province
  who is responsible for occupational health and safety, may jointly
  issue to the Regulator written directives in relation to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the development of guidelines and interpretation
    notes with respect to occupational health and safety matters; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of any recommendations made by
    an auditor under section 205.119 or made following an inquiry under
    section 205.12.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directives binding

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall comply with a directive issued
  under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directives not statutory instruments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Directives issued under this section are not
  statutory instruments for the purposes of the [Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice in [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} When a directive is issued under this section, a
  notice shall be published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) that the directive has been issued
  and that the text of it is available for inspection by any person on
  request made to the Regulator.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 42
- 2014, c. 13, s. 12
- [2024, c. 20, s. 20](#1491096-1529329){.wb-lbx
