  ajax-after="1491291-1530183.html"}
:::

#### [Inquiries]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1530184 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiries

- **[[183.23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.23
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If debris or an accident or incident
  related to any work or activity to which this Division applies occurs
  or is found in any portion of the offshore area and results in death
  or injury or danger to public safety or the environment, the Regulator
  may direct an inquiry to be made and may authorize any person it deems
  qualified to conduct the inquiry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandatory inquiry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If debris or an accident or incident related to any
  work or activity to which this Division applies occurs or is found in
  any portion of the offshore area and is "serious", as defined by
  regulation in accordance with paragraph 183.25(1)(a), the Regulator
  shall direct that an inquiry referred to in subsection (1) be made and
  shall ensure that the person who conducts the inquiry is not employed
  by the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of person conducting inquiry

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of an inquiry under subsection (1),
  a person authorized by the Regulator under that subsection has and may
  exercise all the powers of a person appointed as a commissioner under
  Part I of the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} As soon as feasible after the conclusion of an
  inquiry under subsection (1), the person or persons authorized to
  conduct the inquiry shall submit a report to the Regulator, together
  with the evidence and other material that was before the inquiry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall publish the report within 30 days
  after the Regulator receives it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies of report

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may supply copies of a report published
  under subsection (5) in any manner and on any terms that the Regulator
  considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530235){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530235.html"}
:::

#### [General Duty]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1530236 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonable care

**[[183.24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.24 .sectionLabel}** The holder of an
authorization issued under subsection 138.01(1) shall take all
reasonable care to ensure the safety of persons and facilities and the
protection of property and the environment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530240){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530240.html"}
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-1530241 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[183.25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.25
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 7, the Governor
  in Council may, for the purposes of safety, the protection of the
  environment and accountability, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining, in relation to offshore renewable energy
    projects, "facility" and "equipment" for the purposes of section
    139.1 and "serious" for the purpose of section 183.23;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting works and activities related to offshore
    renewable energy projects;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorizing the Regulator, or any person, to make
    any orders that are specified in the regulations and to exercise any
    powers and perform any duties that are necessary for the design,
    construction, operation or abandonment of an offshore renewable
    energy project within the offshore area;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting arbitrations relating to offshore
    renewable energy projects for the purposes of subsection 138.1(2),
    including the costs of or incurred in relation to such arbitrations;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the approvals to be granted as terms and
    conditions of authorizations issued under subsection 138.01(1);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting, in relation to offshore renewable
    energy projects, the introduction into the environment of
    substances, classes of substances and forms of energy, in prescribed
    circumstances;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the creation, conservation and
    production of records relating to offshore renewable energy
    projects; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} prescribing, in relation to offshore renewable
    energy projects, anything that is required to be prescribed for the
    purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless otherwise provided in this Part, a regulation
  made under subsection (1) may incorporate by reference any document,
  regardless of its source, either as it exists on a particular date or
  as it is amended from time to time.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530256){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530256.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Equivalent standards and exemptions

- **[[183.26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.26
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the Chief
  Safety Officer and Chief Conservation Officer may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} authorize the use of equipment, methods, measures
    or standards in lieu of any required by any regulation made under
    section 183.25 if those Officers are satisfied that the use of that
    other equipment or those other methods, measures or standards would
    provide a level of safety and protection of the environment
    equivalent to that provided by compliance with the regulations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} grant an exemption from any requirement imposed by
    any regulation made under section 183.25 in respect of equipment,
    methods, measures or standards if those Officers are satisfied with
    the level of safety and protection of the environment that will be
    achieved without compliance with that requirement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}One-officer authorizations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer alone may exercise the
  powers referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b) if the regulatory
  requirement referred to in that paragraph does not relate to
  protection of the environment, and the Chief Conservation Officer
  alone may exercise those powers if the regulatory requirement does not
  relate to safety.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No contravention

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person contravenes the regulations if that person
  acts in compliance with an authorization or exemption under
  subsection (1) or (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530267){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530267.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines and interpretation notes

- **[[183.27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.27
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may issue and publish,
  in any manner the Regulator considers appropriate, guidelines and
  interpretation notes with respect to the application and
  administration of section 138.01, any regulations respecting offshore
  renewable energy projects made under sections 29.1 and 183.25 and any
  regulations made under section 183.29 and subsection 183.3(3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed not to be statutory instruments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Guidelines and interpretation notes issued under
  subsection (1) are deemed not to be statutory instruments for the
  purposes of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530274){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530274.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION II.2]{.HLabel2}[Safety and Protection of Persons, Property and the Environment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1530275 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders

- **[[183.28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.28
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator may, by order, direct
  any of the following persons or bodies to take measures in respect of
  an abandoned facility that the Regulator considers necessary for the
  safety of persons or the abandoned facility or for the protection of
  property or the environment:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the holder of an authorization under paragraph
    138(1)(b) or subsection 138.01(1) or any other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a provincial government or a provincial Crown
    corporation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a local authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Measures to be taken

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person or body does not comply with an order
  under subsection (1), the Regulator may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} take any action or measure the Regulator considers
    necessary;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} authorize an officer or employee, or class of
    officers or employees, of the Regulator to take the action or
    measure; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} authorize a third party to take the action or
    measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders deemed not to be statutory
  instruments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Orders made under subsection (1) shall be deemed not
  to be statutory instruments for the purposes of the [Statutory
  Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530290){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530290.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[183.29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.29 .sectionLabel}** Subject to
section 7, the Governor in Council may make regulations respecting
abandoned facilities, including with respect to liability and to the
proof of financial responsibility or financial resources to be provided
by an applicant or holder of an authorization under subsection
138.01(1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530294){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530294.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Abandoned facilities

- **[[183.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person shall not make contact
  with, alter or remove an abandoned facility unless they are authorized
  to do so in an order made under subsection (2) or by regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer may make an order, subject
  to any conditions that that Officer considers appropriate, authorizing
  a person to make contact with, alter or remove an abandoned facility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 7, the Governor in Council may
  make regulations respecting the circumstances in which or conditions
  under which an order under subsection (2) is not necessary.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 76](#1491291-1530303){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491291-1530303.html"}
:::

### [DIVISION III]{.HLabel2}[Appeals and Administration]{.HTitleText2} {#h-154589 .Subheading}

#### [Appeals]{.HTitleText3} {#h-154590 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders and decisions final

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in this Division,
  every decision or order of the Committee is final and conclusive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision or order defined

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any minute or other record of the Committee or any
  document issued by the Committee, in the form of a decision or order,
  shall for the purposes of this section be deemed to be a decision or
  an order of the Committee.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stated case for Supreme Court of
Newfoundland and Labrador

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Committee may of its own motion or at the request of the Regulator
  state a case, in writing, for the opinion of the Supreme Court of
  Newfoundland and Labrador on any question that in the opinion of the
  Committee is a question of law or of the jurisdiction of the
  Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings on case

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Supreme Court of Newfoundland and Labrador shall
  hear and determine the case stated, and remit the matter to the
  Committee with the opinion of the Court on the matter.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 185
- 2014, c. 13, s. 30
- [2024, c. 20, s. 77](#1491293-1530304){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491293-1530304.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-185-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 185"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulator may review orders of Committee

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator may, at
any time, in its discretion, either on petition of any interested person
or of its own motion, vary or rescind any decision or order of the
Committee made under this Part, whether such order is made between
parties or otherwise and any order that the Regulator makes with respect
thereto becomes a decision or order of the Committee and, subject to
section 187, is binding on the Committee and on all parties.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 186
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530955){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530955.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-186-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 186"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Supreme Court of Newfoundland
and Labrador

- **[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  appeal lies from a decision or order of the Committee to the Supreme
  Court of Newfoundland and Labrador on a question of law, on leave
  being obtained from that Court, in accordance with the practice of
  that Court, on application made within one month after the making of
  the decision or order sought to be appealed from or within any further
  time that Court may allow.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Staying order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where leave to appeal is granted pursuant to
  subsection (1), any order of the Committee in respect of which the
  appeal is made shall be stayed until the matter of the appeal is
  determined.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Court

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} After the hearing of the appeal, the Supreme Court of
  Newfoundland and Labrador shall certify its opinion to the Committee
  and the Committee shall make any order necessary to comply with that
  opinion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order subject to section 186

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any order made by the Committee pursuant to
  subsection (3), unless that order has already been dealt with by the
  Regulator pursuant to section 186, shall be subject to that section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 187
- 2014, c. 13, s. 31
- [2024, c. 20, s. 78](#1491298-1530308){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491298-1530308.html"}
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530956){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530956.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-187-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 187"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Operational Safety Officers and Conservation Officers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-154616 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operational safety officers

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the
  Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister shall jointly designate
  as an operational safety officer for the purposes of the
  administration and enforcement of this Part an individual who has been
  recommended by the Regulator. The Ministers shall make the designation
  within 30 days after the day on which they receive the name of the
  individual from the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conservation officers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the Federal Minister and
  the Provincial Minister shall jointly designate as a conservation
  officer for the purposes of the administration and enforcement of this
  Part an individual who has been recommended by the Regulator. The
  Ministers shall make the designation within 30 days after the day on
  which they receive the name of the individual from the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of designation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall, without delay after making a
  designation, notify the Regulator, in writing, that the designation
  has been made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall not designate an individual if
  they are not satisfied that the individual is qualified to exercise
  the powers and carry out the duties and functions of an operational
  safety officer or a conservation officer, as the case may be, under
  this Part. If an individual is not designated, the Ministers shall
  without delay notify the Regulator of it, in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Indemnification

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An individual designated under subsection (1) or (2)
  who is not an employee of the Regulator is deemed to be an officer for
  the purposes of section 16.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 188
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 33
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530959){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530959.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders for verifying compliance

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  operational safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a conservation
  officer or the Chief Conservation Officer may, for the purpose of
  verifying compliance with this Part, order any person in charge of a
  place that is used for any work or activity in respect of which this
  Part applies or any other place in which that officer has reasonable
  grounds to believe that there is anything to which this Part applies

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to inspect anything in the place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to pose questions, or conduct tests or monitoring,
    in the place;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to take photographs or measurements, or make
    recordings or drawings, in the place;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to accompany or assist the officer while the
    officer is in the place;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to produce a document or another thing that is in
    their possession or control, or to prepare and produce a document
    based on data or documents that are in their possession or control,
    in the form and manner that the officer may specify;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} to provide, to the best of their knowledge,
    information relating to any matter to which this Part applies, or to
    prepare and produce a document based on that information, in the
    form and manner that the officer may specify;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} to ensure that all or part of the place, or
    anything located in the place, that is under their control, not be
    disturbed for a reasonable period specified by the officer pending
    the exercise of any powers under this section; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} to remove anything from the place and to provide it
    to the officer, in the manner that he or she specifies, for
    examination, testing or copying.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers on entry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An operational safety officer, the Chief Safety
  Officer, a conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer may,
  for the purpose of verifying compliance with this Part, and subject to
  section 189.2, enter a place that is used for any work or activity in
  respect of which this Part applies or any other place in which that
  officer has reasonable grounds to believe that there is anything to
  which this Part applies, and may for that purpose

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} inspect anything in the place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pose questions, or conduct tests or monitoring, in
    the place;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} take samples from the place, or cause them to be
    taken, for examination or testing, and dispose of those samples;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} remove anything from the place, or cause it to be
    removed, for examination, testing or copying;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} while at the place, take or cause to be taken
    photographs or measurements, make or cause to be made recordings or
    drawings or use systems in the place that capture images or cause
    them to be used;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} use any computer system in the place, or cause it
    to be used, to examine data contained in or available to it;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} prepare a document, or cause one to be prepared,
    based on data contained in or available to the computer system;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} use any copying equipment in the place, or cause it
    to be used, to make copies;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied while in the place by any
    individual, or be assisted while in the place by any person, that
    the officer considers necessary; and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} meet in private with any individual in the place,
    with the agreement of that individual.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Clarification

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, an officer who has entered a
  place under subsection (2) may order any individual in the place to do
  anything described in paragraphs (1)(a) to (h).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of things removed

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Anything removed under paragraph (1)(h) or (2)(d) for
  examination, testing or copying shall, if requested by the person from
  whom it was removed, be returned to that person after the examination,
  testing or copying is completed, unless it is required for the purpose
  of a prosecution under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 189
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 33
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-189-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 189"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reports provided to holder of
authorization

**[[189.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.1 .sectionLabel}** An operational
safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a conservation officer or the
Chief Conservation Officer, as the case may be, shall provide written
reports to the holder of an authorization about anything inspected,
tested or monitored, by or on the order of the officer, for the purpose
of verifying compliance with this Part, at any place that is used for a
work or activity for which the authorization is issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entering living quarters

- **[[189.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the place referred to in
  subsection 189(2) is living quarters

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} neither a conservation officer nor the Chief
    Conservation Officer is authorized to enter those quarters for the
    purpose of verifying compliance with this Part; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an operational safety officer or the Chief Safety
    Officer is not authorized to enter those quarters without the
    consent of the occupant except

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to execute a warrant issued under subsection (4),
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to verify that those quarters, if on a marine
      installation or structure, as defined in subsection 205.001(1),
      are in a structurally sound condition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The officer shall provide reasonable notice to the
  occupant before entering living quarters under subparagraph
  (1)(b)(ii).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Despite subparagraph (1)(b)(ii), any locker in the
  living quarters that is fitted with a locking device and that is
  assigned to the occupant shall not be opened by the officer without
  the occupant's consent except under the authority of a warrant issued
  under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to issue warrant

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} On [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application, a
  justice of the peace may issue a warrant authorizing an operational
  safety officer who is named in it or the Chief Safety Officer to enter
  living quarters subject to any conditions specified in the warrant if
  the justice is satisfied by information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the living quarters are a place referred to in
    subsection 189(2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entry to the living quarters is necessary to verify
    compliance with this Part; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entry was refused by the occupant or there are
    reasonable grounds to believe that entry will be refused or that
    consent to entry cannot be obtained from the occupant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to open locker

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The warrant may also authorize a locker described in
  subsection (3) to be opened, subject to any conditions specified in
  the warrant, if the justice is satisfied by information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is necessary to open the locker to verify
    compliance with this Part; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the occupant to whom it is assigned refused to
    allow it to be opened or there are reasonable grounds to believe
    that the occupant to whom it is assigned will refuse to allow it to
    be opened or that consent to opening it cannot be obtained from that
    occupant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of force

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The officer who executes a warrant issued under
  subsection (4) shall not use force unless the use of force has been
  specifically authorized in the warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Telewarrant provisions to apply

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A warrant may be issued under this section by
  telephone or other means of telecommunication on information submitted
  by an operational safety officer or the Chief Safety Officer by one of
  those means, and section 487.1 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  applies for that purpose, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require.

- Definition of [[living quarters]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[living
  quarters]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means sleeping quarters provided for
  employees, as defined in subsection 205.001(1), on a marine
  installation or structure, as defined in that subsection, and any room
  for the exclusive use of the occupants of those quarters that contains
  a toilet or a urinal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 33
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate to be produced

**[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** The Regulator shall
provide every operational safety officer and conservation officer and
the Chief Safety Officer and the Chief Conservation Officer with a
certificate of appointment or designation and, on entering any place
under the authority of this Part, the officer shall, if so required,
produce the certificate to the person in charge of the place.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 190
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 34(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1530971){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1530971.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-190-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 190"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assistance to officers

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  owner of, and every person in charge of, a place entered by an
  operational safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a conservation
  officer or the Chief Conservation Officer under subsection 189(2), and
  every person found in that place, shall give all assistance that is
  reasonably required to enable the officer to verify compliance with
  this Part and provide any documents, data or information that are
  reasonably required for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transportation, accommodation and food

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the place referred to in subsection 189(2) is a
  marine installation or structure, as defined in subsection 205.001(1),
  the person in charge of the marine installation or structure shall
  provide to the officer, and to every individual accompanying the
  officer, free of charge,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} suitable transportation between the usual point of
    embarkation on shore and the marine installation or structure,
    between the marine installation or structure and the usual point of
    disembarkation on shore, and between marine installations or
    structures, if the marine installation or structure or marine
    installations or structures are situated in the offshore area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} suitable accommodation and food at the marine
    installation or structure.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 191
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-191-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 191"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obstruction of officers and making of
false statements

**[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
obstruct or hinder, or make a false or misleading statement either
orally or in writing to, an operational safety officer, the Chief Safety
Officer, a conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer while
the officer is engaged in carrying out his or her duties or functions
under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 192
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-192-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 192"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Authority to issue warrant

- **[[192.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
  application, a justice of the peace may issue a warrant if the justice
  is satisfied by information on oath that there are reasonable grounds
  to believe that there is in any place anything that will provide
  evidence or information relating to the commission of an offence under
  this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers under warrant

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The warrant may authorize an operational safety
  officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a conservation officer or the Chief
  Conservation Officer, and any other individual named in the warrant,
  to at any time enter and search the place and to seize anything
  specified in the warrant, or do any of the following as specified in
  it, subject to any conditions that may be specified in it:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct examinations, tests or monitoring;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take samples for examination or testing, and
    dispose of those samples; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} take photographs or measurements, make recordings
    or drawings, or use systems in the place that capture images.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where warrant not necessary

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An operational safety officer, the Chief Safety
  Officer, a conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer may
  exercise the powers described in this section without a warrant if the
  conditions for obtaining the warrant exist but by reason of exigent
  circumstances it would not be feasible to obtain one.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exigent circumstances

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Exigent circumstances include circumstances in which
  the delay necessary to obtain the warrant would result in danger to
  human life or the environment or the loss or destruction of evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operation of computer system and
  copying equipment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An individual authorized under this section to search
  a computer system in a place may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any computer system at the
    place to search any data contained in or available to the computer
    system;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} reproduce or cause to be reproduced any data in the
    form of a printout or other intelligible output;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} seize any printout or other output for examination
    or copying; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} use or cause to be used any copying equipment at
    the place to make copies of the data.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of person in charge of place

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is in charge of a place in respect
  of which a search is carried out under this section shall, on
  presentation of the warrant, permit the individual carrying out the
  search to do anything described in subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transportation, accommodation and food

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The person in charge of a marine installation or
  structure, as defined in subsection 205.001(1), shall provide to an
  individual who is executing a warrant under this section at the marine
  installation or structure, free of charge,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} suitable return transportation between the marine
    installation or structure and any location from which transportation
    services to that marine installation or structure are usually
    provided, and between marine installations or structures, if the
    marine installation or structure or marine installations or
    structures are situated in the offshore area; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} suitable accommodation and food at the marine
    installation or structure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Telewarrant provisions to apply

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A warrant may be issued under this section by
  telephone or other means of telecommunication on information submitted
  by an operational safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a
  conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer by one of those
  means, and section 487.1 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
  applies for that purpose, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Storage and removal

- **[[192.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A thing seized under this Part may
  be stored in the place where it was seized or may, at the discretion
  of an operational safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a
  conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer, be removed to
  any other place for storage. The owner of the thing or the person who
  is lawfully entitled to possess it shall pay the costs of storage or
  removal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Perishable things

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the thing seized is perishable, an operational
  safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a conservation officer or
  the Chief Conservation Officer may destroy the thing, or otherwise
  dispose of it in any manner the officer considers appropriate. Any
  proceeds realized from its disposition shall be paid to the Receiver
  General.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 35
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Serious bodily injury

- **[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an operational safety officer or the Chief Safety Officer, on
  reasonable grounds, is of the opinion that continuation of an
  operation in relation to the exploration or drilling for or the
  production, conservation, processing or transportation of petroleum or
  in relation to an offshore renewable energy project in any portion of
  the offshore area is likely to result in serious bodily injury, the
  operational safety officer or Chief Safety Officer, as the case may
  be, may order that the operation cease or be continued only in
  accordance with the terms of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The officer who makes an order under subsection (1)
  shall affix at or near the scene of the operation a notice of the
  order in prescribed form.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry of order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order made by an operational safety officer under
  subsection (1) expires 72 hours after it is made unless it is
  confirmed before that time by order of the Chief Safety Officer.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Modification or revocation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Immediately after an operational safety officer makes
  an order under subsection (1), they shall advise the Chief Safety
  Officer accordingly, and the Chief Safety Officer may modify or revoke
  the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reference to provincial court judge

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The person carrying out the operation to which an
  order under subsection (1) makes reference or any person having a
  pecuniary interest in that operation may by notice in writing request
  the Chief Safety Officer to refer it to a provincial court judge for
  review, and thereupon the Chief Safety Officer shall refer the order
  to a provincial court judge having jurisdiction in the area closest to
  that in which the operation is being carried on.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry by a provincial court judge

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A provincial court judge to whom an order is referred
  pursuant to this section shall inquire into the need for the order and
  for that purpose has all the powers of a commissioner under Part I of
  the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where an order has been referred to a provincial
  court judge pursuant to this section, the burden of establishing that
  the order is not needed is on the person who requested that the order
  be so referred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial court judge's decision
  conclusive

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A provincial court judge to whom an order is referred
  pursuant to this section may confirm or set aside the order and the
  decision of the provincial court judge is final and conclusive.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Operations in respect of which order
  made

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} No person shall continue an operation in respect of
  which an order has been made pursuant to this section, except in
  accordance with the terms of the order or until the order has been set
  aside by a provincial court judge pursuant to this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 193
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 36(E)
- [2024, c. 20, s. 79](#1491303-1530313){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491303-1530313.html"}
:::

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Priority

**[[193.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193.1 .sectionLabel}** An order made by an
operational safety officer or the Chief Safety Officer prevails over an
order made by a conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer
to the extent of any inconsistency between the orders.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 37(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 193.1"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Installation or Facility Manager]{.HTitleText3} {#h-154768 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Installation manager

- **[[193.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every holder of an authorization
  under paragraph 138(1)(b) in respect of a work or activity for which a
  prescribed installation is to be used shall put in command of the
  installation a manager who meets any prescribed qualifications, and
  the installation manager is responsible for the safety of the
  installation and the persons at it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act and any other Act of Parliament,
  an installation manager has the power to do such things as are
  required to ensure the safety of the installation and the persons at
  it, and, more particularly, may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give orders to any person who is at the
    installation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order that any person who is at the installation be
    restrained or removed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} obtain any information or documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In a prescribed emergency situation, an installation
  manager's powers are extended so that they also apply to each person
  in charge of a vessel, vehicle or aircraft that is at the installation
  or that is leaving or approaching it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 35, s. 80
- 2014, c. 13, s. 38
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193.2-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 193.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Facility manager

- **[[193.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every holder of an authorization
  under subsection 138.01(1) in respect of a work or activity for which
  a prescribed facility is to be used shall put in command of the
  facility a manager who meets any prescribed qualifications, and the
  facility manager is responsible for the safety of the facility and the
  persons at it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act and any other Act of Parliament,
  a facility manager has the power to do anything that is required to
  ensure the safety of the facility and the persons at it and, more
  particularly, may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give orders to any person who is at the facility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order that any person who is at the facility be
    restrained or removed; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} obtain any information or documents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emergency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In a prescribed emergency situation, a facility
  manager's powers are extended so that they also apply to each person
  in charge of a vessel, vehicle or aircraft that is at the facility or
  that is leaving or approaching it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 20, s. 81](#1491307-1530327){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491307-1530327.html"}
:::

#### [Offences and Penalties]{.HTitleText3} {#h-154781 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences

- **[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person is guilty of an offence
  who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contravenes this Part or the regulations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} makes any false entry or statement in any report,
    record or document required by this Part or the regulations or by
    any order made under this Part or the regulations;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} destroys, mutilates or falsifies any report or
    other document required by this Part or the regulations or by any
    order made under this Part or the regulations;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} produces any petroleum from a pool or field under
    the terms of a unit agreement within the meaning of Division II, or
    any amended unit agreement, before the unit agreement or amended
    unit agreement is filed with the Chief Conservation Officer;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} undertakes or carries on a work or activity without
    an authorization under paragraph 138(1)(b) or without complying with
    the approvals or requirements, determined by the Regulator in
    accordance with the provisions of this Part or granted or prescribed
    under this Part, of an authorization issued under that paragraph;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} undertakes or carries on a work or activity
    without an authorization under subsection 138.01(1) or without
    complying with the terms and conditions of the authorization that
    were determined by the Regulator in accordance with the provisions
    of this Part or granted or prescribed under this Part; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} fails to comply with a direction, requirement or
    order of an operational safety officer, the Chief Safety Officer, a
    conservation officer or the Chief Conservation Officer or with an
    order of an installation manager, a facility manager or the
    Committee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is guilty of an offence under
  subsection (1) is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding one
    hundred thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    one year, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine not
    exceeding one million dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
    exceeding five years, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentencing principles

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the principles and factors that the
  court is otherwise required to consider, including those set out in
  sections 718.1 to 718.21 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the
  court shall consider the following principles when sentencing a person
  who is found guilty of an offence under this Part:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the fine should be increased to
    account for every aggravating factor associated with the offence,
    including the aggravating factors set out in subsection (4); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the fine should reflect the gravity
    of each aggravating factor associated with the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating factors

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The aggravating factors are the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offence caused harm or risk of harm to human
    health or safety;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the offence caused damage or risk of damage to the
    environment or to environmental quality;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the offence caused damage or risk of damage to any
    unique, rare, particularly important or vulnerable component of the
    environment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the damage or harm caused by the offence is
    extensive, persistent or irreparable;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the offender committed the offence intentionally or
    recklessly;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the offender failed to take reasonable steps to
    prevent the commission of the offence;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} by committing the offence or failing to take action
    to prevent its commission, the offender increased their revenue or
    decreased their costs or intended to increase their revenue or
    decrease their costs;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the offender has a history of non-compliance with
    federal or provincial legislation that relates to safety or
    environmental conservation or protection; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} after the commission of the offence, the offender

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} attempted to conceal its commission,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} failed to take prompt action to prevent,
      mitigate or remediate its effects, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} failed to take prompt action to reduce the risk
      of committing similar offences in the future.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of aggravating factor

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} The absence of an aggravating factor set out in
  subsection (4) is not a mitigating factor.

- Meaning of [[damage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4.2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraphs (4)(b) to (d),
  [[damage]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes loss of use value and non-use
  value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(4.3)]{.lawlabel} If the court is satisfied of the existence of one
  or more of the aggravating factors set out in subsection (4) but
  decides not to increase the amount of the fine because of that factor,
  the court shall give reasons for that decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence defence

  [(4.4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be found guilty of an offence under
  this Part if they establish that they exercised due diligence to
  prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption against waste

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person commits an offence under subsection 154(1)
  by reason of committing waste as defined in paragraph 154(2)(f) or (g)
  unless that person has been ordered by the Committee to take measures
  to prevent the waste and has failed to comply.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 194
- 1992, c. 35, s. 81
- 2014, c. 13, s. 39
- 2015, c. 4, ss. 64, 117
- [2024, c. 20, s. 82](#1491309-1530333){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491309-1530333.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-194-20160227.html "Link to previous version of section 194"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence by officers, etc., of corporation

- **[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  corporation commits an offence under this Part, any of the following
  individuals who directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced in or
  participated in the commission of the offence is a party to and guilty
  of the offence and is liable on conviction to the punishment provided
  for the offence, whether or not the corporation has been prosecuted or
  convicted:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an officer, director or agent of the corporation;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other individual exercising managerial or
    supervisory functions in the corporation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence by employee or agent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In a prosecution for an offence under this Part, it
  is sufficient proof of the offence to establish that it was committed
  by an employee or agent of the accused, whether or not the employee or
  agent is identified or has been prosecuted for the offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 195
- 1992, c. 35, s. 82
- 2014, c. 13, s. 40
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-195-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 195"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imprisonment precluded in certain cases

**[[195.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195.1 .sectionLabel}** If an individual is
convicted of an offence under this Part on proceedings by way of summary
conviction, no imprisonment may be imposed in default of payment of any
fine imposed as punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 40
:::
