instituted at any time within but not later than three years after the
day on which the subject-matter of the proceedings arose, unless the
prosecutor and the defendant otherwise agree.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

**[[205.114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.114 .sectionLabel}** In any
prosecution for an offence under this Part, a copy of any order or other
document purporting to have been made under this Part --- and purporting
to have been signed, in the case of an order or other document
purporting to have been made by the provincial labour relations board,
by the chief executive officer or a member of that board and, in any
other case, by the individual authorized under this Part to make that
order or document --- is, in the absence of any evidence to the
contrary, proof of the matters set out in it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of justice or judge

**[[205.115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.115 .sectionLabel}** Any complaint
or information relating to an offence under this Part may be heard,
tried or determined by a justice of the peace or judge if the accused is
resident or carrying on business within the territorial jurisdiction of
that justice or judge although the matter of the complaint or
information did not arise in that territorial jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action to enjoin not prejudiced by
prosecution

- **[[205.116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.116
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Even though a prosecution has been
  instituted for an offence under this Part, the Regulator may commence
  and maintain an action to enjoin the committing of any contravention
  of any provision of this Part or of the regulations made under this
  Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil remedy not affected

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No civil remedy for any act or omission is suspended
  or affected by reason that the act or omission is an offence under
  this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1531070){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1531070.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.116-20141203.html "Link to previous version of section 205.116"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information

**[[205.117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.117 .sectionLabel}** In any
proceedings for an offence under this Part

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an information may include more than one offence
  committed by the same person;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} all those offences may be tried concurrently; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} one conviction for any or all offences so included
  may be made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

### [Advisory Council]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156470 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment

- **[[205.118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.118
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An advisory council is established,
  composed of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} four representatives of employees and four
    representatives of industry;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two representatives of the government of Canada and
    two representatives of the government of the Province; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Safety Officer [ex officio]{.otherLang
    lang="la"} or his or her representative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of employee and industry
  representatives

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Two of the employee representatives and two of the
  industry representatives shall be appointed jointly by the Federal
  Minister and the Minister of Labour and the other four shall be
  appointed jointly by the provincial counterparts of those Ministers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before making any appointment referred to in
  subsection (2), the Federal Minister and the Minister of Labour, or
  their provincial counterparts, as the case may be, shall consult with
  non-management employees, or the unions representing them, on the
  appointment of an employee representative and with industry
  associations on the appointment of an industry representative.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of government
  representatives

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The federal government representatives shall be
  appointed jointly by the Federal Minister and the Minister of Labour
  and the provincial government representatives shall be appointed
  jointly by the provincial counterparts of those Ministers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mandate

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The mandate of the advisory council is to advise the
  Regulator, the Federal Minister, the Minister of Labour and the
  provincial counterparts of those Ministers on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the administration and enforcement of this Part;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other matter related to occupational health and
    safety that is referred to it by any of them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remuneration and expenses

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} At the discretion of the Federal Minister, the
  Minister of Labour and their provincial counterparts, the members of
  the advisory council may be paid

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the remuneration that may be jointly fixed by those
    Ministers and their provincial counterparts; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any reasonable travel and living expenses that are
    incurred by the members while carrying out their duties or functions
    away from their ordinary place of residence.

  The Regulator shall pay that remuneration and those expenses.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration and renewal of appointments

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Members are to be appointed for a term of not more
  than five years and may be reappointed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Chairpersons

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The advisory council is to have two chairpersons
  selected from among its members. One of the chairpersons shall be
  selected by the employee representatives and the other shall be
  selected by the industry representatives.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1531072){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1531072.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.118-20141203.html "Link to previous version of section 205.118"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Audits and Inquiries]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156498 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of auditor

- **[[205.119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.119
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister or the
  Provincial Minister, or both, may appoint any individual as auditor to
  measure and report on the effectiveness of the Regulator in carrying
  out its duties and functions under this Part. A report of the audit
  shall be made, as soon as practicable, to each of those Ministers and
  the Regulator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The auditor is entitled to free access at all
  convenient times to information that relates to the fulfilment of
  their responsibilities and is also entitled to require and receive
  from the Regulator and from any persons or committees having duties or
  functions under this Part any information --- including reports ---
  and explanations that they consider necessary for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiries

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The auditor may examine any individual on oath on any
  matter pertaining to the effectiveness of the Regulator in carrying
  out its duties and functions under this Part and, for the purposes of
  an examination, may exercise all the powers of a commissioner under
  Part I of the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Secondary release of information

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Information --- including reports --- and
  explanations disclosed to the auditor under subsection (2) shall not
  be further disclosed by the auditor without the consent in writing of
  the person to whom it relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of report

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall consider the report of the audit
  and, within 60 days after the day on which the Regulator receives the
  report, it shall send to the auditor its written response to the
  report, and send a copy of that response to the Federal Minister and
  the Provincial Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cost of audit

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If the Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister
  jointly appoint the auditor, they may also jointly agree, with the
  consent in writing of the Provincial Minister as defined in section 2,
  to require the cost of the audit to be borne by the Regulator. If only
  one of those Ministers appoints the auditor, that Minister shall pay
  the cost of the audit.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1531075){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1531075.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.119-20141203.html "Link to previous version of section 205.119"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiries

- **[[205.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister, the Provincial
  Minister, the Federal Minister jointly with the Provincial Minister or
  the Regulator may appoint one or more individuals to inquire into and
  report on occupational health and safety matters that are related to
  employment to which this Part applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of appointee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An individual who is appointed by the Federal
  Minister, by the Federal Minister jointly with the Provincial Minister
  or by the Regulator has all the powers of a person appointed as a
  commissioner under Part I of the [Inquiries Act](/eng/acts/I-11), and
  the powers that may be conferred on a commissioner under section 11 of
  that Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Witnesses

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every witness who attends and gives evidence at an
  inquiry under this section is entitled to be paid reasonable travel
  and living expenses incurred by the witness in doing so and the
  witness fees prescribed in the tariff of fees in use in the Supreme
  Court of Newfoundland and Labrador.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Once the Regulator receives a copy of the report, it
  shall consider the report and shall, within 60 days after the day on
  which it is received, send to the Federal Minister and the Provincial
  Minister its written response to the report.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions to Regulator

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If one or more individuals are appointed by a
  Minister or Ministers under subsection (1) in respect of a matter, the
  Minister or Ministers making the appointment may, if that Minister or
  those Ministers determine that an inquiry is being conducted under
  section 165 in respect of the same matter, direct that the Regulator
  terminate that inquiry and provide to that individual or those
  individuals any records or evidence collected in respect of the
  matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directions binding

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Regulator shall comply with a direction made
  under subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs of inquiry

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister
  jointly appoint the individual or individuals under subsection (1),
  they may also jointly agree, with the consent in writing of the
  Provincial Minister as defined in section 2, to require the cost of
  the inquiry to be borne by the Regulator. If only one of those
  Ministers appoints an individual or individuals under that subsection,
  that Minister shall pay the cost of the inquiry.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
- [2024, c. 20, s. 99](#1491352-1530442){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491352-1530442.html"}
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1531085){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1531085.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.12-20141203.html "Link to previous version of section 205.12"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Documents in Electronic or Other Form]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156531 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[205.121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.121 .sectionLabel}** The definitions
in this section apply in this section and sections 205.122 and 205.123.

[[electronic document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[electronic document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any form of
    representation of information or of concepts fixed in any medium in
    or by electronic, optical or other similar means and that can be
    read or perceived by an individual or by any means. ([document
    électronique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[information system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[information system]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a system used to
    generate, send, receive, store or otherwise process an electronic
    document. ([système d'information]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use not mandatory

**[[205.122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.122 .sectionLabel}** No provision of
this Part or of the regulations made under this Part requires an
electronic document to be created or provided.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Creation of information in writing

- **[[205.123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.123
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A requirement under this Part that a
  notice, document or other information be created in writing is
  satisfied by the creation of an electronic document if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the information in the electronic document is
    accessible so as to be usable for subsequent reference; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the regulations pertaining to this subsection, if
    any, have been complied with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provision of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A requirement under this Part that a notice, document
  or other information be provided under this Part, whether or not it is
  required to be provided in writing, is satisfied by the provision of
  an electronic document if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the addressee has designated an information system
    for the receipt of the electronic document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the electronic document is provided to the
    designated information system, unless otherwise prescribed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the information in the electronic document is
    accessible by the addressee and capable of being retained by them,
    so as to be usable for subsequent reference; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the regulations pertaining to this subsection, if
    any, have been complied with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for consent

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a consent is required to be given in writing under
  this Part, the requirement is satisfied by the provision of an
  electronic document that signifies that consent has been given if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the addressee has designated an information system
    for the receipt of the electronic document;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the electronic document is provided to the
    designated information system, unless otherwise prescribed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the information in the electronic document that
    signifies that consent has been given is accessible by the addressee
    and capable of being retained by them, so as to be usable for
    subsequent reference; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the regulations pertaining to this subsection, if
    any, have been complied with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (2), the reasons referred to in
  subsection 205.059(5) and the decision referred to in subsection
  205.099(3) shall be provided in writing.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156563 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[205.124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.124
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 7 and on the
  recommendation of the Federal Minister and the Minister of Labour, the
  Governor in Council may make regulations generally for carrying out
  the purposes and provisions of this Part, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishing requirements in respect of anything
    described in subsection 205.015(2) or 205.02(2);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the manner in which an operator is
    required to investigate under subsection 205.017(2) any occupational
    disease or any accident, incident or other hazardous occurrence;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the establishment, by an operator, of
    procedures for safe entry to or exit from a marine installation or
    structure and of standards for occupancy of a marine installation or
    structure;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the establishment of codes of practice,
    and specifying who is responsible for ensuring that those codes of
    practice are complied with;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the safety of work or activities that
    are carried out in a confined space, at heights, directly over
    water, under water, or of any work or activity that involves the use
    of explosives;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting ergonomic standards and procedures for a
    workplace;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the establishment of standards for the
    design, installation and maintenance of the following things:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} guards, guard-rails, barricades, fences and other
      equipment of a similar nature,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} boilers and pressure vessels,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} escalators, elevators and other devices of a
      similar nature,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} all equipment for the generation, distribution
      or use of electricity,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} gas-burning or oil-burning equipment or other
      heat-generating equipment, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} heating, ventilation and air-conditioning
      systems;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} respecting the establishment of standards for the
    design and maintenance of equipment, machines, devices, materials
    and other things that may be used by employees in carrying out their
    job functions;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} respecting the circumstances and manner in which
    any thing referred to in paragraph (g) or (h) shall or shall not be
    used, and any qualifications that an individual is required to have
    in order to use it;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} specifying who is responsible for ensuring that the
    standards referred to in paragraphs (g) and (h) are complied with
    and that the things referred to in those paragraphs are used in the
    specified circumstances and manner and by individuals who have the
    required qualifications;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} respecting the establishment of standards relating
    to levels or limits for ventilation, lighting, temperature,
    humidity, sound and vibration and exposure to chemical agents,
    biological agents and radiation and specifying who is responsible
    for ensuring that those standards are complied with;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} respecting the qualifications of persons authorized
    to carry out prescribed training;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} respecting the establishment of fire safety and
    emergency measures, and specifying who is responsible for ensuring
    that those measures are complied with;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} respecting the provision, by an operator, an
    employer, or both, of sanitary and personal facilities, potable
    water, sustenance, and first-aid and health services;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} respecting the prevention of, and protection
    against, violence at the workplace;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} respecting the manner and form in which records are
    to be maintained and information communicated;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} respecting the manner in which programs for medical
    monitoring and examination referred to in paragraph 205.066(1)(f)
    are to be implemented, including restricting the types of
    interventions that may be used;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} respecting the procedures governing the granting of
    a permission under section 205.069 or 205.07, including any
    requirements for consultation or notice;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} specifying the equipment, methods, measures or
    standards or other things required by regulations made under this
    section in respect of which the granting of a permission under
    section 205.069 or 205.07 is prohibited;

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} respecting the operation of an advisory council
    established under section 205.118;

  - [(u)]{.lawlabel} respecting any matter necessary for the purposes of
    the application of section 205.123, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the time and circumstances when, and the place
      where, an electronic document, as defined in section 205.121, is
      to be considered to have been provided or received, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which a secure electronic
      signature, as defined in subsection 31(1) of the [Personal
      Information Protection and Electronic Documents
      Act](/eng/acts/P-8.6), is required to be linked to an electronic
      document, as defined in section 205.121; and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that by this Part is to be
    prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation by reference

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) may incorporate
  any material by reference, regardless of its source, either as it
  exists on a particular date or as amended from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No registration or publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, a document that is
  incorporated by reference into a regulation is not required to be
  transmitted for registration or published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) by reason only that it is
  incorporated by reference.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations general or specific

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) may be made
  applicable to all persons or one or more classes of persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendation of Minister of Transport

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) in respect of
  employees and other passengers on a passenger craft, or the passenger
  craft, shall, in addition to the requirement set out in that
  subsection, be made on the recommendation of the Minister of
  Transport.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 45
:::

**[[205.125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205.125 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
2024, c. 20, s. 100\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-205.125-20141203.html "Link to previous version of section 205.125"){.PITLink}
:::

## [PART IV]{.HLabel1}[Revenue Sharing]{.HTitleText1} {#h-156614 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156615 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[Consumption Tax Acts]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Consumption Tax Acts]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means section 2 and
    Parts I, II, III, VIII and IX of and the schedule to the Revenue
    Administration Act, S.N.L. 2009, c. R-15.01, as amended from time to
    time, and any other Act of the Legislature of the Province, as
    amended from time to time, that may be prescribed. ([lois sur la
    taxe à la consommation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Insurance Companies Tax Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Insurance Companies Tax Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means section 2
    and Parts I, II and VI of and the schedule to the Revenue
    Administration Act, S.N.L. 2009, c. R-15.01, as amended from time to
    time. ([Loi sur l'imposition des compagnies
    d'assurances]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Newfoundland and Labrador Income Tax Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Newfoundland and Labrador Income Tax
    Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Income Tax Act, 2000, S.N.L.
    2000, c. I-1.1, as amended from time to time. ([Loi de l'impôt sur
    le revenu de Terre-Neuve-et-Labrador]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[Revenue Fund]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Revenue Fund]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the account established
    under section 214. ([Fonds de recettes]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 206
- 2014, c. 13, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-206-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 206"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Imposition of Consumption Taxes]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156624 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imposition of consumption taxes in
offshore area

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be imposed, levied and
  collected under this Part in respect of the offshore area, in
  accordance with subsection (3), the taxes, interest and penalties that
  would be imposed, levied and collected under the Consumption Tax Acts
  if the offshore area were in the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if taxes are imposed in
  respect of any matter under any of the Consumption Tax Acts and taxes
  would, but for this subsection, be imposed under subsection (1) in
  respect of that matter, no taxes shall be imposed under subsection (1)
  in respect of that matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Newfoundland and
  Labrador legislation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act and the regulations made under
  it, the Consumption Tax Acts and the regulations referred to in
  subsection (3.1) apply, with any modifications that the circumstances
  require, for the purposes of this Part and, without limiting the
  generality of the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference in those Acts to the Crown is to be
    read as a reference to Her Majesty in right of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference in those Acts to the province is to be
    read as a reference to the offshore area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reference in those Acts to the Minister
    responsible for the administration of any of those Acts is to be
    read as a reference to the Minister of Finance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Newfoundland and Labrador regulations

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The following regulations apply for the purposes of
  subsection (3):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any regulations made under the Consumption Tax
    Acts; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any regulations made under section 107, 108, 111,
    112 or 114 of the Revenue Administration Act, S.N.L. 2009, c.
    R-15.01, as that Act is amended from time to time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding on certain entities

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section is binding on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the corporations mentioned in Schedule I to the
    [Federal-Provincial Fiscal Arrangements Act](/eng/acts/F-7.8), if
    Newfoundland and Labrador is a participating province as defined in
    subsection 31(1) of that Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the corporations mentioned in Schedule II to that
    Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 207
- 2014, c. 13, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Imposition of Insurance Companies Tax]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156645 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Insurance companies tax in offshore area

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be imposed, levied and
  collected under this Part in respect of the insurance premiums
  received by any company with respect to property that is situated in
  the offshore area at the time the insurance premiums become payable,
  in accordance with subsection (3), the taxes, interest and penalties
  that would be imposed, levied and collected under the Insurance
  Companies Tax Act if the property were situated in the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if taxes are imposed in
  respect of any matter under the Insurance Companies Tax Act and taxes
  would, but for this subsection, be imposed under subsection (1) in
  respect of that matter, no taxes shall be imposed under subsection (1)
  in respect of that matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Newfoundland and
  Labrador legislation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act and the regulations made under
  it, the Insurance Companies Tax Act and any regulations made under
  section 107 of the Revenue Administration Act, S.N.L. 2009, c.
  R-15.01, as that Act is amended from time to time, apply, with any
  modifications that the circumstances require, for the purposes of this
  Part and, without limiting the generality of the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference in that Act to the Crown is to be read
    as a reference to Her Majesty in right of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference in that Act to the province is to be
    read as a reference to the offshore area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reference in that Act to the Minister responsible
    for the administration of that Act is to be read as a reference to
    the Minister of Finance.

- Definition of [[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has
  the same meaning as in the Insurance Companies Tax Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 208
- 2014, c. 13, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-208-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 208"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Tax Administration Agreement]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156660 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to collect

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 212(1), where
  a tax administration agreement is entered into pursuant to subsection
  (3), taxes, interest, penalties and other sums payable under section
  207 or 208 may be collected and administered and refunds in respect
  thereof may be granted by the Government of the Province on behalf of
  the Government of Canada in accordance with the terms and conditions
  of the agreement, as amended from time to time pursuant to subsection
  (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negotiation of tax administration
  agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Finance shall, on the request of the
  Government of the Province, negotiate with the Provincial Minister of
  Finance a tax administration agreement with respect to the taxes,
  interest and penalties imposed under sections 207 and 208.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tax administration agreement

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} On completion of the negotiation of a tax
  administration agreement pursuant to subsection (2), the Minister of
  Finance, with the approval of the Governor in Council, shall, on
  behalf of the Government of Canada, enter into a tax administration
  agreement with the Government of the Province pursuant to which the
  Government of the Province shall, on behalf of the Government of
  Canada, collect and administer the taxes, interest, penalties and
  other sums payable under sections 207 and 208 and, without limiting
  the generality of the foregoing, grant refunds or make other payments
  in respect of those taxes, interest and penalties in accordance with
  the terms and conditions set out in the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments to the agreement

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Finance, with the approval of the
  Governor in Council, may, on behalf of the Government of Canada, enter
  into an agreement amending the terms and conditions of any tax
  administration agreement entered into pursuant to subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No further liability

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A tax administration agreement may provide that,
  where any payment is received by the Government of the Province on
  account of any taxes, interest, penalties or other sums payable by a
  person under

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 207 or 208, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} both

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} section 207 or 208, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Consumption Tax Acts or the Insurance
      Companies Tax Act,

  the payment so received may be applied by the Government of the
  Province towards the taxes, interest, penalties or other sums payable
  by the person under any such provision or Act in such manner as is
  specified in the agreement, notwithstanding that the person directed
  that the payment be applied in any other manner or made no direction
  as to its application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any payment or part thereof applied by the Government
  of the Province in accordance with a tax administration agreement
  towards the taxes, interest, penalties or other sums payable by a
  person under section 207 or 208

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} relieves that person of liability to pay such
    taxes, interest, penalties or other sums to the extent of the
    payment or part thereof so applied; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall be deemed to have been applied in accordance
    with a direction made by that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of provision of tax
  administration agreement

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to be an agreement entered into
  pursuant to subsection (3) or (4) that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} certified as such by, or on behalf of, the Receiver
    General, the Deputy Receiver General or the Minister of Finance

  is, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, evidence of the
  contents thereof and is admissible in evidence without proof of the
  signature or official character of the person purporting to have
  certified it.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 209
- 2014, c. 13, s. 47
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-209-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of powers and duties

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a tax administration agreement
  is entered into, the Provincial Minister, on behalf of, or as agent
  for, the Minister of Finance, is hereby authorized to perform all the
  duties and to exercise all the powers and any discretion that the
  Minister of Finance or the Deputy Minister of Finance has under this
  Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of powers and duties

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a tax administration agreement is entered into,
  the Minister of Finance of the Province may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} perform the duties and exercise any power or
    discretion that the Provincial Minister has under subsection (1) or
    otherwise under this Part; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} designate officers of the Department of Finance of
    the Province to carry out any functions, perform any duties and
    exercise any powers that are similar to those that are carried out,
    exercised or performed by them on behalf of the Provincial Minister
    under the Consumption Tax Acts and the Insurance Companies Tax Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 210
- 2014, c. 13, s. 48
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-210-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 210"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Imposition of Corporate Income Taxes]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156697 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imposition of corporate income tax in
offshore area

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be imposed, levied and
  collected under this Part in respect of the taxable income of a
  corporation earned in a taxation year in the offshore area, in
  accordance with subsection (3), the taxes, interest and penalties that
  would be imposed, levied and collected under the Newfoundland and
  Labrador Income Tax Act in respect of that taxable income if the
  offshore area were in the Province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if taxes are imposed under
  the Newfoundland and Labrador Income Tax Act in respect of taxable
  income of a corporation earned in a taxation year in the Province and
  taxes would, but for this subsection, be imposed under subsection (1)
  in respect of that taxable income, no taxes shall be imposed under
  subsection (1) in respect of that taxable income.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Newfoundland and
  Labrador Income Tax Act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Act and the regulations made under
  it, the Newfoundland and Labrador Income Tax Act and any regulations
  referred to in subsection (3.1) apply, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require, for the purposes of this Part and, without
  limiting the generality of the foregoing,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference in that Act to Her Majesty in right of
    the Province of Newfoundland and Labrador is to be read as a
    reference to Her Majesty in right of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference in that Act to the province is to be
    read as a reference to the offshore area; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reference in that Act to the "Minister of
    Finance" is to be read as a reference to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in relation to the remittance of any amount as or
      on account of tax payable under that Act, the Receiver General for
      Canada, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in relation to any other matter, the Minister of
      National Revenue for Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Newfoundland and Labrador regulations

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The following regulations apply for the purposes of
  subsection (3):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any regulations made under the Newfoundland and
    Labrador Income Tax Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any regulations made under an Act that was replaced
    by the Newfoundland and Labrador Income Tax Act, to the extent that
    those regulations remain in force in accordance with the laws of the
    Province and are not inconsistent with the Newfoundland and Labrador
    Income Tax Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of taxable income earned
  in offshore area

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the taxable income
  of a corporation earned in a taxation year in the offshore area or in
  the Province shall be determined in accordance with rules prescribed
  by regulations for the purposes of the definition [[taxable income
  earned in the year in a province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection
  124(4) of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 211
- 2014, c. 13, s. 49
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-211-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 211"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remittance to Receiver General

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} All
  taxes, interest, penalties or other sums payable under section 207,
  208 or 211 are payable and shall be remitted to the Receiver General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consolidated Revenue Fund

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the collection or receipt of any taxes, interest,
  penalties or other sums by the Government of the Province pursuant to
  this Part, the taxes, interest, penalties or other sums shall be
  deposited as soon as practicable to the credit of the Receiver General
  and paid into the Consolidated Revenue Fund in the manner prescribed
  by the Treasury Board under the [Financial Administration
  Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Collection of excess sums

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who knowingly collects or receives any
  sum of money as taxes under this Part in circumstances where the sum
  is not payable shall forthwith

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} refund the sum to the person from whom he collected
    it; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person referred to in paragraph (a) is not
    known or readily ascertainable, pay the sum to the Receiver General.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 212
- 2015, c. 4, s. 67(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-212-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 212"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Liability and Collection of Tax]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156729 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Debts due to Her Majesty

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** All taxes, interest,
penalties or other sums payable under section 207, 208 or 211 are debts
due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and are recoverable as such from
the person required to pay the taxes, interest, penalties or other sums
in accordance with this Part.

### [Revenue Fund]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156732 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revenue Fund

- **[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} There shall be established in the
  accounts of Canada an account to be known as the Newfoundland Offshore
  Petroleum Resource Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change of name

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The name of the Newfoundland Offshore Petroleum
  Resource Revenue Fund is changed to Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore
  Petroleum Resource Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} A reference in any contract, document, instrument,
  proclamation or order in council to the Newfoundland Offshore
  Petroleum Resource Revenue Fund is, unless the context otherwise
  requires, to be read as a reference to the Newfoundland and Labrador
  Offshore Petroleum Resource Revenue Fund.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Federal Minister shall, at the times and in the
  manner prescribed,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} credit the Revenue Fund with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the Government of the Province has agreed
      to collect and is collecting in accordance with a tax
      administration agreement entered into pursuant to section 209, on
      behalf of the Government of Canada, taxes, interest, penalties and
      other sums payable under sections 207 and 208, an amount equal to
      the aggregate of the amounts on account of taxes, interest,
      penalties and other sums collected pursuant to those sections
      during any fiscal year, after taking into account any refunds,
      remissions or other payments that are applicable in respect of
      such taxes, interest, penalties and other sums,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to the aggregate of the amounts
      assessed or reassessed in respect of any fiscal year on account of
      taxes imposed pursuant to section 211, after taking into account
      any credits, reductions, deductions, rebates, surtaxes and
      remissions that are applicable in respect of such taxes,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} where the Government of the Province and the
      Regulator have entered into an agreement pursuant to section 98
      and the Regulator is collecting, in accordance with the agreement,
      on behalf of the Government of Canada, royalties payable under
      section 97, an amount equal to the aggregate of such royalties
      that have been collected during any fiscal year, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to the aggregate of any amounts,
      other than those referred to in subparagraph (iii), received and
      not required to be returned during any fiscal year under Part II
      or any regulations made thereunder; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pay to Her Majesty in right of the Province, at the
    time and in the manner prescribed, any amount credited to the
    Revenue Fund pursuant to paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Excess recoverable

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where, pursuant to subsection (2), Her Majesty in
  right of the Province has received any amount in excess of the amount
  to which it is entitled, the Federal Minister may recover as a debt
  due to Her Majesty in right of Canada an amount equal to such excess
  from any moneys that may be or may become payable to Her Majesty in
  right of the Province pursuant to subsection (2) or under any other
  Act of Parliament.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 214
- 2014, c. 13, s. 50
- [2024, c. 20, s. 101](#1491355-1531097){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1491355-1531097.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-214-20141231.html "Link to previous version of section 214"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Jurisdiction of Courts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156752 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of courts

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every court in the Province has
  jurisdiction in respect of matters arising in the offshore area under
  this Part or Division VI of Part II or under any laws made applicable
  by this Part or that Division to the offshore area, to the same extent
  as the court has jurisdiction in respect of matters arising within its
  ordinary territorial division.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Presumption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the offshore area
  shall be deemed to be within the territorial limits of the judicial
  centre of St. John's.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section limits the jurisdiction that
  a court may exercise apart from this section.

- Definition of [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  includes a judge thereof and any provincial court judge or justice.

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, on the recommendation of the Minister of Finance, make regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} excluding, for the purposes of this Act, any
  provision or any part of a provision of the Consumption Tax Acts, the
  Newfoundland and Labrador Income Tax Act, the Insurance Companies Tax
  Act or the Petroleum and Natural Gas Act as defined in subsection
  97(1) or of any regulation referred to in subsection 97(4.1),
  207(3.1), 208(3) or 211(3.1) that is inconsistent with this Act, the
  Atlantic Accord or any bilateral or international treaty, convention
  or agreement respecting taxation, tariffs or trade to which the
  Government of Canada is a signatory; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that is by this Part to be
  prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1987, c. 3, s. 216
- 2014, c. 13, s. 51
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Appropriation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156769 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appropriation

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** In respect of any
fiscal year, the Federal Minister may pay out of the Consolidated
Revenue Fund, at such times and in such manner as may be prescribed,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount to Her Majesty in right of the Province on
  account of amounts that have been refunded or reimbursed during that
  fiscal year by the Government of the Province to taxpayers in respect
  of taxes paid under this Part; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} amounts payable during that fiscal year to Her
  Majesty in right of the Province pursuant to paragraph 214(2)(b).

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Corporate Income Tax]{.HTitleText1} {#h-156793 .Part}

**[[234 and 235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234_and_235
.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments\]

## [PART VIII]{.HLabel1}[Transitional, Consequential and Commencement]{.HTitleText1} {#h-156795 .Part}

### [Transitional]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156796 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Initial payment to environmental studies
fund during transitional period

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding section
49 of the Canada Oil and Gas Act as it read immediately before the
coming into force of this section, where an exploration agreement in
relation to any portion of the offshore area was entered into or
negotiations in respect thereof were completed under the Canada Oil and
Gas Act on or after February 11, 1985 and before the coming into force
of Part VII of the [Canada Petroleum Resources Act](/eng/acts/C-8.5),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the interest owner shall deposit for payment into the
  relevant fund an amount determined in accordance with subsection 81(2)
  of the [Canada Petroleum Resources Act](/eng/acts/C-8.5); and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where, prior to the coming into force of this
  section, there has been deposited for payment into the relevant fund
  in relation to that exploration agreement an amount determined in
  accordance with section 49 of the Canada Oil and Gas Act as it read
  immediately before the coming into force of this section, the Minister
  may refund to the interest owner an amount equal to the difference
  between that amount and the amount required to be deposited under
  paragraph (a).

### [Consequential Amendments]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156801 .Subheading}

**[[237 and 238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237_and_238
.sectionLabel}** \[Amendments to other Acts\]

### [Coming into Force]{.HTitleText2} {#h-156803 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Coming into force

- **[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#C-7.5_en_1){#C-7.5_en_1-ID0EBDA}[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), this Act
  or any provision thereof shall come into force on a day or days to be
  fixed by proclamation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 211, 234 and 235 are applicable to taxation
  years commencing after April 4, 1987.

  - ::: Footnote
    [[Return to footnote
    ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#C-7.5_en_1-ID0EBDA){#C-7.5_en_1}\[Note: Act,
    except Division VIII of Part II and sections 207 and 208, in force
    April 4, 1987, *see* SI/87-88; Division VIII of Part II, in force
    May 20, 1988, *see* SI/88-102; sections 207 and 208, in force
    January 23, 1996, *see* SI/96-20.\]
    :::

## RELATED PROVISIONS {#related-provisions .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2014, c. 13, s. 53, as amended by 2018, c. 27, s. 179

  - ###### [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5)  --- transitional regulations {#canadanewfoundland-and-labrador-atlantic-accord-implementation-act-transitional-regulations .MarginalNote}

    - [53]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} If any of the following documents
      are transmitted by the Minister of Natural Resources to the
      Speaker of the Senate and to the Speaker of the House of Commons
      for tabling in those Houses during the 2nd session of the 41st
      Parliament, then on the day on which this section comes into force
      that document is deemed to be a regulation made under subsection
      205.124(1) of the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic
      Accord Implementation Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) and, despite section 9
      of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22), to have come
      into force on that day:

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a document entitled Canada--Newfoundland and
        Labrador Offshore Marine Installations and Structures
        Occupational Health and Safety Transitional Regulations; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a document entitled Canada--Newfoundland and
        Labrador Offshore Marine Installations and Structures
        Transitional Regulations.

    - ###### [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5)  --- transitional regulations {#canadanewfoundland-and-labrador-atlantic-accord-implementation-act-transitional-regulations-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a document entitled Canada--Newfoundland and
      Labrador Offshore Area Diving Operations Safety Transitional
      Regulations is transmitted by the Minister of Natural Resources to
      the Speaker of the Senate and to the Speaker of the House of
      Commons for tabling in those Houses during the 2nd session of the
      41st Parliament, then on the day on which this section comes into
      force

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the document is deemed to be a regulation made
        under paragraph 205.001(3)(a) and subsection 205.124(1) of the
        [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord
        Implementation Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) and, despite section 9 of
        the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22), to have come
        into force on that day; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the [Newfoundland Offshore Area Petroleum
        Diving Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-88-601) are repealed.

    - ###### Approval of provincial ministers {#approval-of-provincial-ministers .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} The documents are to be transmitted to the
      Speaker of the Senate and to the Speaker of the House of Commons
      only if the Minister of Natural Resources is satisfied that, in
      the case of those referred to in paragraphs (1)(a) and (b), the
      minister of the government of Newfoundland and Labrador who is
      responsible for occupational health and safety has approved them
      and, in the case of a document entitled Canada--Newfoundland and
      Labrador Offshore Area Diving Operations Safety Transitional
      Regulations, that provincial minister and the minister designated
      by that government as the provincial minister for the purposes of
      the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord
      Implementation Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5), have approved it.

    - ###### Copies to Clerk {#copies-to-clerk .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Natural Resources shall, within
      seven days after the coming into force of this section, transmit
      to the Clerk of the Privy Council copies of each document referred
      to in subsections (1) and (2) that the Minister of Natural
      Resources transmitted to the Speaker of the Senate and to the
      Speaker of the House of Commons. Section 5 of the [Statutory
      Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22) is deemed to have been complied
      with in respect of a document referred in subsection (1) or (2) on
      the day on which that document is transmitted to the Clerk.

    - ###### Repeal {#repeal .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} Unless repealed on an earlier date, the
      Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore Marine Installations
      and Structures Occupational Health and Safety Transitional
      Regulations, the Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore Marine
      Installations and Structures Transitional Regulations and the
      Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore Area Diving Operations
      Safety Transitional Regulations are repealed on the expiry of six
      years after the day on which this section comes into force.

- --- 2014, c. 13, s. 53.1, as amended by 2021, c. 10, par. 3(b)

  - ###### Revival of transitional regulations {#revival-of-transitional-regulations .MarginalNote}

    - [53.1]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Canada--Newfoundland and
      Labrador Offshore Marine Installations and Structures Occupational
      Health and Safety Transitional Regulations, the
      Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore Marine Installations
      and Structures Transitional Regulations and the
      Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore Area Diving Operations
      Safety Transitional Regulations, as they read immediately before
      they were repealed on December 31, 2020, are revived on January 1,
      2021.

    - ###### Repeal {#repeal-1 .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations that are revived under
      subsection (1) are repealed on December 31, 2021, unless they are
      repealed on an earlier date following their revival under this
      section.

    - ###### Offences {#offences .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence under
      a provision of a regulation revived under subsection (1) if the
      offence was committed during the period beginning on January 1,
      2021 and ending on the day before the day on which this section
      comes into force.

- --- 2014, c. 13, s. 54

  - ###### Chief Safety Officer powers {#chief-safety-officer-powers .MarginalNote}

    - [54]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer may, on
      application, grant an exemption in respect of a workplace, or
      grant an exemption to an operator in respect of passengers being
      transported on a passenger craft to or from any of its workplaces,
      for a specified time and subject to specified conditions, from any
      requirement in respect of equipment, methods, measures or
      standards that is set out in the Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador
      Offshore Marine Installations and Structures Occupational Health
      and Safety Transitional Regulations, the Canada--Newfoundland and
      Labrador Offshore Marine Installations and Structures Transitional
      Regulations or the Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Offshore Area
      Diving Operations Safety Transitional Regulations, if he or she is
      satisfied that the health and safety of employees at the workplace
      or passengers on the passenger craft, as the case may be, will be
      maintained without compliance with the requirement.

    - ###### No contravention {#no-contravention .MarginalNote}

      [(2)]{.lawlabel} The regulations are not considered to be
      contravened if there is compliance with an exemption under
      subsection (1).

    - ###### Application {#application .MarginalNote}

      [(3)]{.lawlabel} The application shall

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in a form acceptable to the Chief Safety
        Officer;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} include information with respect to the
        consequences to health and safety that might reasonably be
        anticipated if the exemption is granted; and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied by technical information
        sufficient to enable the Chief Safety Officer to make a decision
        on the application.

    - ###### Public notice {#public-notice .MarginalNote}

      [(4)]{.lawlabel} On receipt of the application, the Chief Safety
      Officer shall make it available to the public in a manner that he
      or she considers advisable, together with a notice that
      submissions may be made to him or her for a period of 30 days ---
      or any shorter period that he or she fixes with the agreement of
      the applicable workplace committee --- after the day on which the
      application has been made available.

    - ###### Notice at workplace, etc. {#notice-at-workplace-etc. .MarginalNote}

      [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the application is made in respect of an
      existing workplace, the applicant shall give a copy of the
      application to the operator. An operator shall, immediately after
      it receives or makes an application relating to an existing
      workplace or to a passenger craft

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} post a copy of it in printed form in a
        prominent place at the applicable workplace; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provide a copy to any committee established for
        that workplace and to any union representing employees within
        the offshore area.

    - ###### Decision {#decision .MarginalNote}

      [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer shall, as soon as
      possible after the end of the period referred to in subsection
      (4), give to the applicant and the operator a copy of the decision
      made on the application, and make the decision available to the
      public in a manner that he or she considers advisable.

    - ###### Reconsideration of decision {#reconsideration-of-decision .MarginalNote}

      [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Chief Safety Officer may, on his or her own
      initiative or on application by the applicant for the exemption
      under subsection (1), reconsider, confirm, vary, revoke or suspend
      a decision made on the application at any time if information is
      made available that, had it been known when the decision was made,
      would reasonably be expected to have resulted in a different
      decision from the one made at that time. In that case,
      subsections (1) to (6) apply with the necessary modifications.

    - ###### Same meaning {#same-meaning .MarginalNote}

      [(8)]{.lawlabel} Words and expressions used in this section have
      the same meaning as in subsection 205.001(1) of the
      [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation
      Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5).

- --- 2024, c. 20, s. 104

  - ###### Definitions {#definitions .MarginalNote}

    [104]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in sections 105 and
    106.

    [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[Board]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section
        2 of the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord
        Implementation and Offshore Renewable Energy Management
        Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) as it read immediately before the day on
        which subsection 3(1) of this Act comes into
        force. ([Office]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

    [[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

    :   [[Regulator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
        section 2 of the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic
        Accord Implementation and Offshore Renewable Energy Management
        Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5), as it reads on the day on which
        subsection 3(3) comes into force. ([Régie]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

- --- 2024, c. 20, s. 105

  - ###### Continuation of members' terms {#continuation-of-members-terms .MarginalNote}

    [105]{.lawlabel} Every person who is a member of the Board
    immediately before the day on which section 3 comes into force
    continues as a member of the Regulator for the remainder of their
    term.

- --- 2024, c. 20, s. 106

  - ###### Continuation of employees' employment {#continuation-of-employees-employment .MarginalNote}

    [106]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Act is to be construed as affecting
    the status of an employee who occupied, immediately before the day
    on which section 3 comes into force, a position with the Board,
    except that the employee, beginning on that day, occupies that
    position with the Regulator.
:::

## AMENDMENTS NOT IN FORCE {#amendments-not-in-force .scheduleTitleText}

- --- 2024, c. 20, ss. 220(1), (9), (11)

  - ###### [2019, c. 28]{.HistoricalNote} {#c.-28 .MarginalNote}

    - [220]{.lawlabel} [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[other
      Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means An Act to enact the Impact
      Assessment Act and the Canadian Energy Regulator Act, to amend the
      Navigation Protection Act and to make consequential amendments to
      other Acts, chapter 28 of the Statutes of Canada, 2019.

    - [(9)]{.lawlabel} On the first day on which both subsection 2(4) of
      the other Act and section 62.1 of this Act are in force,
      subsections 138.012(5) and (6) of the [Canada--Newfoundland and
      Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation and Offshore Renewable
      Energy Management Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) are replaced by the
      following:

      :::: section
      ::: AmendedText
      - ###### Comments for Agency --- time limits {#comments-for-agency-time-limits .MarginalNote}

        [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Agency decides that an impact assessment
        of the designated project is required, the Chairperson of the
        Regulator shall provide the Agency with comments respecting the
        time limits referred to in subsection 37.1(1) of the [Impact
        Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) within which the impact
        assessment report shall be submitted to the Minister of the
        Environment and within which any recommendations shall be posted
        on the Internet site established by the Agency.

      - ###### Comments --- time extension {#comments-time-extension .MarginalNote}

        [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Chairperson of the Regulator shall provide
        the Minister of the Environment with comments respecting any
        extension to the time limits that is considered in accordance
        with subsections 37(3) or (4) and 37.1(4) of the [Impact
        Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75).
      :::
      ::::

    - [(11)]{.lawlabel} On the first day on which both section 7 of the
      other Act and section 62.1 of this Act are in force,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 44 of the [Canada--Newfoundland and
        Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation and Offshore Renewable
        Energy Management Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) is amended by adding the
        following after subsection (2):

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Commissioner appointed from roster {#commissioner-appointed-from-roster .MarginalNote}

          [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A commissioner appointed under paragraph
          (2)(b) may be a person selected under subparagraph
          50(1)(d)(ii) of the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75).
        :::
        ::::

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic
        Accord Implementation and Offshore Renewable Energy Management
        Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) is amended by adding the following after
        section 138.012:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Terms of reference --- Consultation {#terms-of-reference-consultation .MarginalNote}

          [138.0121]{.lawlabel} If the Minister of the Environment
          consults the Chairperson of the Regulator respecting the
          establishment of a review panel's terms of reference under
          subsection 48.1(1) of the [Impact Assessment
          Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75), the Chairperson shall consult the
          Federal Minister and the Provincial Minister.
        :::
        ::::

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} The [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic
        Accord Implementation and Offshore Renewable Energy Management
        Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) is amended by adding the following after
        section 138.013:

        :::: section
        ::: AmendedText
        - ###### Consultation --- selection of members to roster {#consultation-selection-of-members-to-roster .MarginalNote}

          [138.0131]{.lawlabel} If the Minister of the Environment
          consults the Federal Minister respecting the selection of any
          member of the Regulator to a roster under subparagraph
          50(1)(d)(i) of the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75),
          the Federal Minister shall consult with the Provincial
          Minister and the Chairperson of the Regulator.

        - ###### Consultation --- selection of persons to roster {#consultation-selection-of-persons-to-roster .MarginalNote}

          [138.0132]{.lawlabel} If the Minister of the Environment
          consults the Federal Minister and the Regulator respecting the
          selection of any other person to a roster under subparagraph
          50(1)(d)(ii) of the [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75),
          the Regulator shall consult with the Federal Minister and the
          Provincial Minister.

        - ###### Consultation with Provincial Minister --- referral to Governor in Council {#consultation-with-provincial-minister-referral-to-governor-in-council .MarginalNote}

          [138.0133]{.lawlabel} If the Minister of the Environment
          consults the Federal Minister under subsection 61(1) of the
          [Impact Assessment Act](/eng/acts/I-2.75) with respect to the
          referral to the Governor in Council of an impact assessment
          report for a designated project, the Federal Minister shall
          consult the Provincial Minister.
        :::
        ::::
:::
